How to read this file — critical minerals

Same method as the copper and precious-metals tabs

  • 2nd 3×? (the chip on each card): after a first 3× in years 1–5, is there room for another 3× by about year 10? Yes = a believable path (big or growing resource, several assets, growth paid from cash flow); partly = capped or dependent on the metal price; little = the first re-rating is most of the value. Judged per share at today's market value. It is not about cash: months of cash left are on the card's Financials tab (where it exists) and in the funding line. Full explanation on the rankings page under table B.
  • Financials (tab on the card, pilot on 9 companies so far): the last 8 quarters and 3 fiscal years read from the original financial statements on the company's own website (no data feed), simplified to the lines that matter — cash, burn, months of cash left, what went into the ground vs overhead, money raised and share count, and for producers revenue, margin and free cash flow. Every column links to the PDF it came from; every quarter is checked (opening cash + net flow = closing cash). The findings feed the runway and risk notes; there is no separate score.
  • Management (chip on each card, full review in the card's Management tab): the people graded A–D on track record (what they built, sold or ran before, misses included), fit (have they done this exact job: same stage, deposit type, region) and candour (a dated promises-vs-delivery log). A★ tier 1 · B solid, or unproven but clean — the default · C real weaknesses · D a pattern of failing shareholders. Neutral by default: the odds move only with evidence, full weight for explorers and developers, half for producers. A fourth score, skin in the game (1–5, in the Management tab), is the team's own money at risk — a conviction signal that never moves the odds but breaks near-ties in list B; a team that gets paid while shareholders lose scores 1. Reviewed 5 Oct 2026, refreshed every Saturday.
  • Bucket (the colour bar): cheap defined resources needs at least an Indicated resource plus a study; grade / discovery is drill-bit optionality; quality / jurisdiction is de-risked and priced for it; scale & financing is a big asset whose gate is the cheque; producer / cash flow holds names with revenue, valued on EV/EBITDA and margin. Processor / midstream (critical minerals only) is a company whose case is a separation, refining or conversion plant rather than a deposit: it has no resource to value, so the anchor is EV per tpa of capacity, the contracted share of capacity, and margin per kg, and the risk screen is technology scale-up, feed security, offtake/price and funding. Its rankings columns use EV/EBITDA on run-rate EBITDA at each price case where a defensible figure exists.
  • Gate, differentiator, Funding & next catalyst panel (funded / partly / not funded to the next dated event), risk 1–5 (chance of permanent loss or no re-rate inside 3–5 years) and the Rule screen (after-tax IRR ≥ 25–30% at a conservative price, NPV ≥ 2–3× capex, payback ≲ 3 years) mean exactly what they mean on the other two tabs.
  • Shareholder structure is shown, never scored. News links to the company's own releases.
  • Filing rule: a company sits where its primary investment case sits. Aftermath (manganese), Rua Gold (antimony) and Osisko Metals' critical-minerals spin-out stay on their current tabs.

What changes for critical minerals

  • Mineral group badge beside each name — Battery (Li, Ni, graphite, Co, Mn), Defence / strategic (Sb, W, rare earths, Ga/Ge, Sc), Uranium — with the specific mineral as the badge text and a matching filter row.
  • The anchor depends on the mineral: lithium — EV per tonne LCE by confidence category, and which product (spodumene concentrate vs carbonate/hydroxide); uranium — EV per lb U3O8 by category, and term vs spot price; graphite, nickel, cobalt — EV per tonne of contained product, read against whether the product is qualified with a battery-maker; rare earths — NdPr (and Dy/Tb) content, because the basket price is mostly magnet metals; antimony, tungsten, Ga/Ge — there is often no transparent price, so the anchor is the offtake contract.
  • Policy is a price input, not background. China dominates processing for most of these minerals and has used export controls on gallium, germanium, antimony and rare earths. Western price floors, government offtakes and agency funding (US DoD/DPA, EXIM, Canada, the EU Critical Raw Materials Act) can decide both the price a project gets and how it is financed. Every card states which policy lever it depends on and how durable that lever is.
  • Processing is often the real gate. A resource is not a product: battery-grade qualification, a separation circuit (rare earths) or a conversion plant (lithium hydroxide) can cost more and take longer than the mine. The gate names which step is missing.
  • Uranium reads differently: utilities buy on long-term contracts, so a producer's contract book (volumes, floors, ceilings) matters as much as spot; ISR vs conventional mining decides capex and timing; enrichment and conversion are separate bottlenecks.

Research shorthand, not investment advice. Prices for many of these minerals are thin, opaque or policy-set — read anchors as shapes, not decimal-precise rankings. Rankings: Critical minerals tab on the rankings page — certainty vs multi-bagger, grows with this tab.

Australia's first ore-to-oxide NdPr project, fully funded by sovereign and strategic money and now in construction — priced at a 52-week low because NdPr sits ~US$20–40/kg under its study deck

Gate Build a first-of-its-kind Australian ore-to-oxide plant for US$1.19B within the US$462M of overrun cover, and ramp it by 2031 — while NdPr recovers to the deck The money is in place; the gate is execution. Nolans is a mine plus a sulphuric-acid plant plus a cracking-and-leach circuit plus solvent extraction, all on a remote NT site, with a 37-month build, a two-year ramp and a contingency that was cut from US$140M to US$87M between the 2022 and 2025 estimates. Lynas took years to ramp a simpler flowsheet. Sub-gates: (1) debt contractual close and EFA/GRMF settlement in October 2026; (2) earthworks done by mid-2027 and the major contracts awarded within the capex estimate; (3) first product late 2029 and nameplate by late 2031/early 2032 without drawing beyond the US$160M cost-overrun account and US$200M standby facility; (4) NdPr back above ~US$117/kg, the offtake-period deck, by the time the first index-linked contracts bill — at US$95 the project earns ~US$290M EBITDA against ~US$855M of senior debt; (5) a new chair and a new CFO settling in at the start of the build.

Funded to the next catalyst Company says fully funded through construction with no further shareholder equity: ~A$1.07B equity raised or committed + A$200M NRFC + US$1,055M of debt facilities against a US$1.9B total requirement (US$1.6B excl. the undrawn overrun and standby facilities). No gap today; the residual risk is an overrun beyond US$462M of cover, which would mean more equity at construction-stage prices, and the fact that the debt has not yet reached contractual close.
Thesis

Arafura is building Nolans, 135 km north of Alice Springs: a 38-year mine and integrated processing plant making 4,440 tpa of NdPr oxide, 573 tpa of SEG/HRE oxide and 144 kt of phosphoric acid. FID was taken on 21 May 2026 after ~A$1.07B of equity (A$481M at A$0.28 in Dec 2025, A$361.5M at A$0.26 in May–Jul 2026, A$230M from EFA and Germany's GRMF pending settlement) plus a A$200M NRFC convertible note and US$775M of ECA-led senior debt (EFA, NAIF, EDC, KEXIM, KfW IPEX/Euler Hermes-covered banks); contractual close of the debt is targeted for October 2026. Binding offtake covers ~3,650 tpa (~82% of nameplate: Hyundai/Kia 1,500, Siemens Gamesa 520, Traxys 800, India 500, a global trading company 850 since 12 Oct) priced off a seaborne index, plus a non-binding A$1.2B Critical Minerals Strategic Reserve letter for 500. At A$0.145 (US$607M, 15 Oct; 52-week high A$0.62) the market pays ~0.33× the US$1,859M base NPV8 — but that NPV needs US$117–136/kg NdPr and today's price is ~US$95–97; at a flat US$95 the NPV is roughly US$600–700M and the stock is at ~1× NAV.

The Gate

Build a first-of-its-kind Australian ore-to-oxide plant for US$1.19B within the US$462M of overrun cover, and ramp it by 2031 — while NdPr recovers to the deck The money is in place; the gate is execution. Nolans is a mine plus a sulphuric-acid plant plus a cracking-and-leach circuit plus solvent extraction, all on a remote NT site, with a 37-month build, a two-year ramp and a contingency that was cut from US$140M to US$87M between the 2022 and 2025 estimates. Lynas took years to ramp a simpler flowsheet. Sub-gates: (1) debt contractual close and EFA/GRMF settlement in October 2026; (2) earthworks done by mid-2027 and the major contracts awarded within the capex estimate; (3) first product late 2029 and nameplate by late 2031/early 2032 without drawing beyond the US$160M cost-overrun account and US$200M standby facility; (4) NdPr back above ~US$117/kg, the offtake-period deck, by the time the first index-linked contracts bill — at US$95 the project earns ~US$290M EBITDA against ~US$855M of senior debt; (5) a new chair and a new CFO settling in at the start of the build.

Not a 2 because it is a first-of-kind Australian ore-to-oxide chemical plant on a remote site with a 37-month build, contingency trimmed to US$87M, a price deck US$20–40/kg above the market, the debt not yet contractually closed, and the chair and CFO both gone in the first quarter of construction. Not a 4 because there is no forced raise in sight, no drawn debt, and the Commonwealth is lender, shareholder and customer. Loss path: an overrun in 2028 → equity top-up at A$0.10–0.15 → slow ramp into a US$80–90 NdPr market → the stock trades at 0.3–0.5× a lower NAV for years.

The flowsheet is beneficiation → sulphuric-acid bake → water leach → phosphoric-acid by-product → impurity removal → solvent-extraction separation to NdPr oxide and a SEG/HRE oxide (70% payability on Dy/Tb now assumed). Opex US$46.4/kg NdPr at the mine gate, US$31.9/kg net of the phosphoric-acid credit; C1 US$49.2/kg. Mine life 38 years includes two years of ramp-up and five years of low-grade stockpile at the end. Land: granted mining leases in the Northern Territory, Traditional Owner agreements in place, 3 of 5 EPBC environmental management plans approved at 30 Jun 2026; a 208-bed camp reinstated and a 48-bed fly camp commissioned.

Valuation Anchor

The market is paying ~0.33× the company's base NPV before dilution (US$607M at A$0.145, 15 Oct) — or, on a flat-US$95 NdPr re-run (my arithmetic: ~US$29M of NPV per US$1/kg, so NPV ≈ US$1,859M − 41 × 29 ≈ US$650M), about 1.0× NAV, which is what a funded construction project usually trades at. Peers: Lynas (producer, ~A$0.3–0.4 per kg of capacity... not comparable), Iluka's Eneabba refinery (government-funded, A$1.8B+); the closest comparison is Arafura's own history — Hancock paid A$0.37 (2022), A$0.28 (2025) and A$0.26 (2026) and the stock is below all three. The discount is a price-deck discount, not a funding discount: every US$10/kg on NdPr moves the NPV by ~US$290M, ~50% of the market cap.

Shareholder structure

Hancock Prospecting (Gina Rinehart) 17.48% (1,053M shares, Jul 2026; bought at A$0.37, A$0.28, A$0.26; can go to <20% without a bid). After settlement: Export Finance Australia ~A$146M and KfW/GRMF ~A$84M in new shares (~3–4% each at A$0.26, my estimate; KfW gets a board nominee); NRFC holds a A$200M convertible note (conv. A$0.476). Board and management <0.1%: MD Cuzzubbo ~1.43M shares (~A$235k). Insider buying: Spreadborough 610k at ~A$0.16 (17 Jun 2026), Higgins 500k at ~A$0.17 (30 Jul 2026), Southey and Murray A$30k each in the SPP at A$0.26 (13 Jul 2026); no sales found. Institutions: placements led by Australian and offshore institutions, no single holder >5% identified besides Hancock.

Read: A government-and-Rinehart register: the Commonwealth (EFA equity, NRFC note, NAIF/EFA debt, Strategic Reserve offtake) and Germany now sit beside Hancock, which is three for three on buying above today's price. Management owns almost nothing.

Jurisdiction risk

National: Australia — Tier-1. The Commonwealth is lender, shareholder and customer (EFA US$125M debt + US$100M equity + US$200M standby, NAIF A$150M, NRFC A$200M note, the first use of the A$1.2B Critical Minerals Strategic Reserve for up to 500 tpa, a 10% production tax credit from 2027). Local: Northern Territory — Nolans is the first 'significant project' under the Territory Coordinator Act 2025; mining leases granted; 3 of 5 EPBC Act management plans approved by 30 Jun 2026 (the other two must land before the relevant works); NT royalty 2.5% of revenue; Safeguard Mechanism carbon liability ~US$5.3M/yr; remote site 135 km from Alice Springs with its own thermal power station (44 × 1.5 MW units) and a sulphuric-acid plant, which carries radiation-management and water conditions. Traditional Owners attended the 8 Oct groundbreaking.

Sovereign risk is near zero; the risk is operational — a remote, chemically complex plant in a jurisdiction that has never run one.

Differentiator

The only fully funded, permitted, ore-to-oxide NdPr project outside China and Lynas with sovereign money on all three sides of the table — lender, shareholder and offtaker. 1,500 tpa to Hyundai/Kia, 520 tpa to Siemens Gamesa (extended Sep 2026 to 5+3 years on a seaborne index), 800 tpa to Traxys, 500 tpa to an Indian government-backed scheme and 850 tpa to a global trading company (binding 12 Oct 2026, up from a 500 tpa term sheet): binding ~3,650 tpa (~82% of nameplate) after 12 Oct, plus a non-binding 500 tpa Strategic Reserve letter; none to China. A phosphoric-acid by-product worth ~US$14.5/kg NdPr and a thorium-free ('radiation-free') NdPr oxide help the cost line.

Counterweight: the deck is US$117–136/kg against a US$95–97 market, and a 37-month first-of-kind build has to come in on US$1.19B.

Exploration upside

Not the value driver · moves the needle: low The reserve (29.5 Mt) is about half the resource (56 Mt) and the mine life is already 38 years, so more ore adds nothing to NPV at 8%. The real option is a Phase 2 expansion or an SEG/HRE separation step once the plant runs — neither costed. Exploration spend was A$0.7M in the June quarter.

Next Catalyst

October 2026: contractual close of the US$775M senior debt and settlement of the A$230M EFA/GRMF equity (both guided for the September quarter, now October); 22 Oct AGM with a new chair to be named; ~27 Oct September quarterly (cash, early-works spend). December quarter: bulk-earthworks contract; mid-2027 earthworks complete. First production late 2029.

Open Questions
  • Has the senior debt reached contractual close, and what conditions precedent remain for first drawdown (offtake confirmations, EPBC plans 4 and 5)?
  • At what price are the EFA and GRMF shares being issued, and what is the fully diluted count after them and the NRFC note?
  • What is the price mechanism in the Hyundai/Kia and Siemens Gamesa contracts — pure seaborne index, or a floor/collar — and does the Strategic Reserve letter carry a floor?
  • Why did the chair leave with immediate effect three weeks before the AGM and five months after FID, and who replaces him?
  • How much of the US$1.19B capex is fixed-price under Hatch's EPCM, and where would the ~A$200M of 'design and sequencing' savings mentioned in September come from?
  • What NdPr price and ramp curve do the lenders' base case use, and when does the first debt service fall due relative to first production?
  • Is the 'extended' wind-turbine offtake the same Siemens Gamesa contract, and did the original expire at end-September?
Context & updates
23 Oct 2026 update

AGM on 22 Oct: all resolutions passed, including the share issues to EFA and KfW and the NRF convertible notes, so shareholder approval for the A$230M EFA/GRMF equity is in hand; settlement and the US$775M debt contractual close are still awaited (guided for October). Ian Murray (director since 2024, formerly CEO of Gold Road) is the new chair, which answers the open question of who replaces Mark Southey. Chair address: construction started in September; >80% of nameplate under term sheets or contracts. Odds 13%, risk 3 and management grade B unchanged; the September quarterly (~27 Oct) is next.

10 Oct 2026 added

Added to the tracker at the owner's request. First assessment: risk 3/5, chance it works ~45%, odds of a 3× ~12%, room for a second 3×: partly. Management B.

News releases · July 2026 – October 2026
October 2026
  • 22 OctAppointment of Chair and Company Secretary Change via ASX platformIan Murray, a director since Sep 2024 and audit chair, becomes chair from the close of the 22 Oct AGM, succeeding Mark Southey. The release says he was most recently CEO and MD of Gold Road. An internal appointment of a former mining CEO closes the governance gap the card flagged; grade and odds unchanged.
  • 22 OctResults of Meeting via ASX platformAll seven AGM resolutions carried on a poll, including the share issues to Export Finance Australia and KfW (the A$230M EFA/GRMF equity) and the convertible notes to the National Reconstruction Fund — shareholder approval was one of the steps before those funds settle. Remuneration report 94% for (no strike). Settlement and debt contractual close themselves are still not announced.
  • 12 OctArafura enters binding offtake agreement via Market Index (ASX release summary; company-site copy not found yet)Minor positive: the May term sheet with a global trading company (likely Traxys North America) becomes a full five-year binding contract for 850 tpa NdPr at nameplate (up from 500 tpa) plus heavy rare earths, priced off a seaborne index. Lifts contracted volume a little further toward the lenders' target; the gate (debt close in October, then build and NdPr price) is unchanged. Arafura will stop naming offtakers. Odds unchanged.
  • 09 OctNolans groundbreaking — construction officially commenced (ceremony 8 Oct)Matters as a milestone: ministers, Traditional Owners and financiers on site; early earthworks began Sep, bulk earthworks Dec quarter. No funding update — debt close still pending.
September 2026
  • 18 SepArafura extends binding offtake agreement with global wind-turbine OEMMatters: up to 500 tpa NdPr-equivalent for 5 years + 3-year option on a seaborne index, replacing a contract whose milestone was due to lapse end-September; keeps the lenders' offtake covenant intact. Stock +11.8%.
  • 16 SepChair Mark Southey steps down with immediate effect; will not stand at the 22 Oct AGMMatters: the chair who delivered FID leaves at the start of construction with no successor named; search under way. Governance flag until a chair is appointed.
August 2026
  • 31 AugFY2026 Annual Report — A$723M cash and term deposits, net loss A$41.1M, contractual close targeted Oct 2026Matters: confirms the economics table (NPV8 US$1,859M, capex US$1,191M) and the schedule — practical completion end-2029, nameplate late 2031/early 2032.
July 2026
  • 31 JulJune 2026 Quarterly Activities Report and Appendix 5BMatters: A$723M liquidity, Traxys NA and Indian term sheets (500 tpa NdPr + 7 tpa Dy/Tb each), EFA/GRMF completion guided for the Sep quarter (now Oct), lenders 'completing final credit approvals'.
  • 15 JulHancock Prospecting lifts stake to 17.48%Matters: 326.9M shares at A$0.26 in the two tranches; Hancock can go to just under 20% without a bid.
  • 13 JulShare Purchase Plan results — A$11.5M raised vs A$25M targetMinor: retail under-subscribed for the second raise running (Dec 2025 SPP A$7M vs A$50M); institutions and Hancock carry the equity.
  • 08 JulCFO Peter Sherrington to resign after ~18 years; Angela Bigg appointed CFO from 13 JulMatters a little: an orderly handover (Sherrington stays to 31 Oct) but the second senior change in the quarter the build starts.
  • 02 JulEGM approves share issues to EFA and KfW, NRFC convertible notes, placement tranche 2 and SPPMatters: all resolutions passed on a poll; clears the shareholder-approval condition on the A$430M of cornerstone equity and notes.

Links go to the company's own site · all releases · grey notes are my read of whether a release changes this card.

Management review
Bsolid, or unproven but clean (the default)Track record 3/5 · Fit 3/5 · Candour 3/5 · Skin in the game 2/5

A team that has done the hard part — a A$1.07B equity stack, US$1B of sovereign debt and ~75% offtake — but has never built or run a rare-earth plant, slipped FID by ~18 months with the slips disclosed, and changed chair and CFO at the start of construction. Default B; the new chair (Ian Murray, from 22 Oct 2026) is a former Gold Road CEO; watch the first capex update.

Effect on the odds: none — neutral by default; odds move only with evidence.

People
Darryl Cuzzubbo (Managing Director & CEO, since Early 2023 (exact start date unverified; SimplyWall.st shows conflicting 2.7-yr CEO / 4.9-yr director tenures)): Ex-Managing Director of SolGold plc (to Nov 2022, a short tenure that ended amid shareholder pressure); earlier a senior operations career at BHP (detail unverified). Took Arafura from a stalled 2024 FID target to FID in May 2026 with ~A$1.07B of equity. · Mark Southey (Former chair (stepped down Sep 2026; succeeded by Ian Murray at the 22 Oct AGM), since Director Jan 2018, Chair Feb 2019): Led the board through the 2022 Project Update, the funding stack and FID; left with immediate effect and no successor named. Participated in the Jul 2026 SPP (A$30k). · Angela Bigg (CFO, since 13 Jul 2026): 20+ years in mining, corporate finance and operations per the company; replaces Peter Sherrington (18 years, leaves 31 Oct 2026). · Michael Spreadborough (Non-executive director, since ~2024): Bought 610k shares on market at ~A$0.16 on 17 Jun 2026. · Roger Higgins (Non-executive director, since ~2024): Bought 500k shares on market at ~A$0.17 on 30 Jul 2026. · Catherine Moises (Non-executive director, since ~2019): KfW will get a board nominee on settlement of the GRMF shares. · Ian Murray (Chair (from 22 Oct 2026 AGM), since Director since 10 Sep 2024): Director and audit chair since Sep 2024; most recently CEO and MD of Gold Road (per the 22 Oct 2026 release); non-executive director of Black Rock Mining, chair of Jupiter Mines. Took A$30k in the Jul 2026 SPP.
Track record
  • Cuzzubbo: SolGold MD 2021–Nov 2022 — Cascabel (Ecuador) did not reach a financing; he left after ~18 monthssource
  • Arafura under Cuzzubbo: equity raised A$20M at A$0.16 (Sep 2024), A$80M at A$0.19 (Aug 2025), A$481M at A$0.28 (Dec 2025), A$361.5M at A$0.26 (May–Jul 2026) — each at a 15–17% discount, share count ~2.3B → ~6.07B (https://www.capitalbrief.com/briefing/arafura-shares-dive-after-discounted-placement-c7313126-cc2c-428a-a694-89b05ff6c6e9/; https://rareearthexchanges.com/news/arafura-rare-earths-capital-structure-tightens-as-hancock-prospecting-lifts-stake-to-15-6/)
  • FID reached 21 May 2026 and ground broken 8 Oct 2026 — the first ex-Lynas Australian rare-earth ore-to-oxide build to reach constructionsource
  • Offtake: Hyundai/Kia (Nov 2022), Siemens Gamesa, Traxys Europe, Traxys NA and India (2026), Strategic Reserve letter — ~93% of the 80% binding targetsource
  • Project economics held roughly flat 2022→2025 (capex +5%, NPV +10%) while contingency was cut from US$140M to US$87Msource

Boards today: Cuzzubbo: Arafura only (no other current listed directorships found). The chair seat is vacant pending the 22 Oct AGM; KfW nominee to join on settlement.

Fit to this job
Stage: construction of a mine plus a chemical plant — fits an operations-trained MD and a Hatch EPCM, but nobody on the executive has built a rare-earth separation plant. Commodity: NdPr — the team's edge is in financing and government relations (EFA, NAIF, NRFC, KfW, EDC, KEXIM all landed), not in rare-earth metallurgy. Region: Northern Territory — yes; long-standing Traditional Owner agreements. New for them: running a 37-month build with a fresh chair and a fresh CFO.
Skin & pay
2/5 — tiny MD holding against A$2.7M pay; small on-market director buys at A$0.16–0.17, no selling. MD Cuzzubbo ~1,426,973 shares (~A$235k at A$0.165, <0.03%); took 789k shares in the Aug/Oct 2025 placement at A$0.19 and 187.5k in the 2024 SPP at A$0.16; 10.6M performance rights/shares issued 15 Dec 2025 at A$0.22 (nature unverified). Directors Spreadborough (610k at ~A$0.16, Jun 2026) and Higgins (500k at ~A$0.17, Jul 2026) bought on market; Southey and Murray A$30k each in the SPP. No insider sales found in 12 months. MD total pay A$2.72M (FY2025, SimplyWall.st) against a ~A$1.0B market cap. Sources: https://www.marketindex.com.au/asx/directors/darryl-cuzzubbo; https://simplywall.st/stocks/au/materials/asx-aru/arafura-rare-earths-shares/management

Skin in the game is a signal of the team's own conviction: it never moves the odds, but breaks near-ties in list B of the rankings.

Red flags
  • Chair resigned with immediate effect on 16 Sep 2026, five months after FID and three weeks before the AGM, with no reason and no successor — a governance vacuum at the start of a US$1.2B build.
  • CFO of 18 years leaving in the same quarter construction starts (handover to 31 Oct 2026).
  • MD paid A$2.72M (75% at-risk/equity) and owns ~1.43M shares (~A$235k) — pay is ~12× the value of his holding; pay rose while the company was loss-making.
  • Every raise since 2022 at a 15–17% discount; share count ~2.6× in three years; retail twice under-subscribed its SPP.
  • The 22 May 2026 investor presentation needed an ASX-prompted clarification of the project economics.
  • Contingency cut from US$140M to US$87M in the refreshed estimate for a first-of-kind plant.

What the numbers say
  1. Cash fell from A$42.2M (30 Jun 2024) to A$27.2M (30 Jun 2025, 3.0 quarters of funding) and then jumped to A$723.2M at 30 Jun 2026 (A$223.2M cash + A$500.0M term deposits) after A$748M of gross equity in FY26; a further ~A$187M (placement tranche 2 A$174.5M + SPP A$12.9M) settled in July 2026, giving >A$900M pro forma. No debt has been drawn: the US$775M senior debt / US$1.05bn package (EFA, NAIF, EDC, KEXIM, ECA-covered banks) is still at documentation with financial close targeted for October 2026.
  2. Funding to date is 100% equity: A$24.6M at A$0.16 (Jul-Sep 2024), A$80M at A$0.19 + A$9.8M SPP (Aug-Oct 2025), A$481M at A$0.28 (Nov-Dec 2025, Hancock A$125M), A$350M at A$0.26 + A$12.9M SPP (May-Jul 2026, Hancock A$85M). Shares rose from 2,310M (Jun 2024) to 2,464M (Jun 2025), 5,355M (Jun 2026) and 6,071M (Jul 2026), i.e. +163% in two years, before the pending EFA (US$100M) and German Raw Materials Fund (EUR50M) subscriptions (~A$230M) and the A$200M NRFC convertible notes (conversion price A$0.476).
  3. Pre-FID Nolans spend was expensed through operations: A$100.3M in FY24 (early works, camp), A$23.4M in FY25 and A$18.5M in FY26, plus ~A$2M/yr capitalised E&E and a A$6.75M camp purchase (Mar 2026). Overhead (staff + admin) was A$16.3M FY24, A$13.2M FY25 and A$13.1M FY26 - about 14% of cash spend in FY24 but ~40% in FY25-FY26 when project spend was throttled; it is now rising again (A$4.0M in the Jun-2026 quarter) as the owner's team ramps up, offset by interest income of A$6.9M a quarter on the cash pile.
  4. Net loss was A$101.0M in FY24, A$19.2M in FY25 and A$41.1M in FY26 (H1 FY26 A$13.0M incl. A$3.2M share-based pay; the implied H2 FY26 loss of A$28.1M is well above H2 cash spend, so non-cash items - SBC, FID-related charges - must be large; the detailed FY26 income statement could not be read). Equity went from A$144.1M to A$845.7M; E&E asset is steady at ~A$124M.
  5. With FID taken on 21 May 2026 (pre-production capex US$1,191M, construction start Sep 2026, practical completion late 2029) the Jun-2026 quarter burn of A$7.2M (ops + E&E) is not representative: construction cash calls will dominate from the Sep-2026 quarter, and the cash chain will shift from 'runway' to 'drawdown vs budget'. Unusual items: A$4.1M camp-contract termination cost (Jun 2025 quarter), A$15M of the A$30M Modern Manufacturing Initiative grant forfeited (Mar 2025), A$4M pastoralist compensation settled in shares (Mar 2026).

Nolans project costs were EXPENSED until the FID of 21 May 2026 and appear in the Appendix 5B as operating 'development' payments, so cfo carries the project spend; they are counted as spend.out.ground. Brazil's most advanced ionic-clay NdPr developer: DFS done, US$120M of equity committed (OneIM, Brazilian funds), BNDES money — but US$280M of debt, binding offtake and the Installation Licence are still open before a Q4 2026 FID

Gate Financial close in Q4 2026: ~US$280M of senior debt signed, a binding Solvay offtake, the Installation Licence — then a 5 Mtpa ionic-clay plant built and ramped in Brazil by H2 2028 The equity is largely in hand, so the gate is now the debt stack and the paper that lenders need: binding offtake with a price mechanism, an Installation Licence from FEAM, an Independent Technical Report, and credit approvals from EFA/EDC/Bpifrance/BNDES-scale lenders. Lender term sheets were targeted for end-September 2026 and the Solvay binding agreement for Q3 2026; neither had been announced as of 15 October. FID has already moved from Q3 2026 (Dec 2025 guidance) to 2H 2026 to 'financial close in Q4 2026'. After FID the test becomes execution: only Serra Verde has built an ionic-clay operation in Brazil, and the ammonium-sulphate leach at this scale is new to the country's regulators.

Partly funded to the next catalyst Equity: US$135M needed, US$154M identified (US$120M new commitments + US$14M cash + US$20M ORE/Régia tranches 3–4). Of that, A$118M is banked; OneIM tranche 2 (A$44.8M) needs FIRB and the 4 Nov vote; ORE/Régia US$20M is at VWAP−5% with an A$1.50 floor. Debt: ~US$280M of the US$449M capex has no signed facility yet — ECA letters (EFA up to US$50M, EDC up to US$100M, Bpifrance) are non-binding. Funded through FID and early works; not funded to build without the debt. A further equity top-up is likely if the debt comes in below 70% or capex grows.
Thesis

Colossus is a 5 Mtpa ionic-clay mine and ammonium-sulphate leach plant at Poços de Caldas that would make ~4,450 t/yr of refined mixed rare-earth carbonate (≈2,840 t NdPr oxide, ~125 t Dy+Tb) for 25 years from a 200 Mt reserve inside a 473 Mt resource — the highest-grade Measured IAC magnet-REO resource outside China by the company's own claim. The August 2026 DFS (Hatch) shows after-tax NPV8 US$1.20B and 36% IRR on US$449M capex, C1 US$9.84/kg REO, and the equity slice (US$135M) is covered by US$120M of new commitments led by Rajeev Misra's OneIM plus Vale/BNDES-appointed Brazilian funds. What is not done: ~US$280M of senior debt (term sheets were due end-September), a binding Solvay offtake (was due Q3), the Installation Licence, and a first-of-its-kind-in-Brazil flowsheet built on time. At A$3.11 (15 Oct) the market pays ~0.29× the DFS NAV before dilution — a fair price for a project this close to FID with that many open items, and the NAV itself sits on a NdPr deck (US$129) above today's China price.

The Gate

Financial close in Q4 2026: ~US$280M of senior debt signed, a binding Solvay offtake, the Installation Licence — then a 5 Mtpa ionic-clay plant built and ramped in Brazil by H2 2028 The equity is largely in hand, so the gate is now the debt stack and the paper that lenders need: binding offtake with a price mechanism, an Installation Licence from FEAM, an Independent Technical Report, and credit approvals from EFA/EDC/Bpifrance/BNDES-scale lenders. Lender term sheets were targeted for end-September 2026 and the Solvay binding agreement for Q3 2026; neither had been announced as of 15 October. FID has already moved from Q3 2026 (Dec 2025 guidance) to 2H 2026 to 'financial close in Q4 2026'. After FID the test becomes execution: only Serra Verde has built an ionic-clay operation in Brazil, and the ammonium-sulphate leach at this scale is new to the country's regulators.

Risk score3/5A DFS with a reserve, a Preliminary Licence, a demo plant running above design recoveries, US$118M of equity banked at A$3.79 from investors who did three months of diligence, BNDES approval and no debt — this is a project that gets sold or re-financed, not abandoned. Not a 2 because the US$280M debt, the binding offtake and the Installation Licence are all unsigned, the two dated September targets have already passed in silence, FID has slipped twice, federal prosecutors are on record against the licensing, NdPr pricing is opaque and the DFS deck (US$129) sits above China's price, and nobody has built an ammonium-sulphate ionic-clay plant at 5 Mtpa in Brazil before. Not a 4 because the equity is in, the stock is ~0.29× NAV, and the next twelve months are funded. Loss path: term sheets slip into 2027 → OneIM tranche 2 conditions fail or the debt comes in at 50% → an equity top-up of US$80–100M at A$2.50–3.00 (+15–20% shares) → MPF action on the IL → stock at ~A$1.80–2.20 (−45–55%).
Market cap15 Oct 2026: ~US$346M (A$3.11 × 161.1M shares = A$501M); EV ~US$255M after est. cash. 52-week range A$0.87–4.58; ~US$241M in Feb 2026; +173% over 12 months. Shares +60% YoY: ORE/Régia tranche 1 at A$0.91 (Nov 2025, 8.4M shares), 31.1M placement shares at A$3.79 (Aug 2026), ORE/Régia tranche 2 at A$3.38. Still to come: OneIM tranche 2 up to 12.1M shares (≥A$3.70), ORE/Régia tranches 3–4 (US$20M at VWAP−5%, floor A$1.50), 3.45M performance rights (2.1%).
Capex / IRRCapex US$405M + contingency = US$449M (AACE Class 3, competitive tenders) — up 25% from the PFS's US$358M (Jul 2025) and the scoping study's US$373M. After-tax IRR 36.4% at US$129 NdPr; pre-tax 47%. PFS after-tax IRR was 34% at US$90 NdPr.
NPVAfter-tax NPV8 US$1,196M (pre-tax US$1,866M) at NdPr US$129/kg spot; US$1,190M at the Western-floor deck. PFS Jul 2025: after-tax NPV8 US$899M at US$90 base, US$481M at US$63 spot. Scoping (early 2025): US$388M after-tax.

Reserve (Aug 2026): 200.1 Mt @ 2,894 ppm TREO / 715 ppm MREO, 579 kt contained TREO, 41 years at 5 Mtpa; the DFS values only a 25-year production target at a 3,061 ppm feed grade. Recoveries: Northern Concessions 76% MREO / 64% TREO; demonstration plant (100 kg/h, Poços de Caldas) averaged 78.1% MREO in July 2026. Product: refined MREC after lanthanum removal — 63.9% NdPr, 2.8% Dy+Tb of REO (PFS: 37.4% / 1.5%). Northern Concessions expanded 46% in late 2025; Southern Complex outside the plan.

Valuation Anchor

Developer anchor: P/NAV on the DFS. US$346M (A$3.11, 15 Oct) against after-tax NPV8 US$1,196M = ~0.29× (~0.21× on EV) — in the range the market pays for a DFS-stage project with equity but no debt signed (0.3–0.5×). Each US$10/kg of NdPr is worth ~US$125M of after-tax NPV (2,841 t × 75% payability × 64% after tax × 9.1 PV factor), so at US$90 the NAV is ~US$710M and the stock is ~0.49× — the discount is mostly a NdPr-price discount, not an execution discount. Nearest peer is Meteoric Resources (ASX: MEI, Caldeira, 25 km away, same MPF opposition) — its market cap was not checked here.

Shareholder structure

OneIM (Rajeev Misra's fund) 9.9% (15.95M shares at A$3.79, 31 Aug 2026; tranche 2 would lift it to ~15%). Verde Serviços Internacionais 5.1% (8.24M — likely the ORE/Régia vehicle, unverified). CSHG Graal Fund (Credit Suisse Hedging-Griffo, Brazil) bought 5.83M shares (A$20.6M, ≤A$3.53) on 30 Sep 2026 → substantial holder notice 5 Oct. JGP Asset Management 2.7% (cornerstone of the Jul 2025 raise at A$0.91; sold ~5.6M shares between Feb and Aug 2026 at A$1.42–5.48). Firetrail 1.7%. Insiders ~9.5% in total per Simply Wall St but board ~4.5%: Exec Chair Pervez 3.6% (5.79M), CEO Moreno 0.71% (1.14M), Rosado 0.16%, Silberman 5,000 shares, Bedford none. Institutions ~6%, retail ~68%.

Read: A register that turned Brazilian and institutional in 2026 — Vale/BNDES-linked funds, OneIM and CSHG bought at A$3.4–3.8 while the early cornerstone (JGP) sold up; the board holds little beyond Pervez's placement stock.

Jurisdiction risk

National: supportive. Brazil's R$5B BNDES/FINEP strategic-minerals call selected Viridis (Jul 2025), BNDES approved R$77.5M (Sep 2026) and is 'working on larger-scale financing'; the ORE/Régia cornerstone funds are the Vale/BNDES-appointed managers of the Strategic Mineral Fund; the EU's international-partnerships commissioner visited Colossus (Jun 2026); ECA letters from Australia, Canada and France. Local: contested. Federal prosecutors (MPF) recommended on 25 Nov 2025 that Copam pull Colossus and Meteoric's Caldeira from its agenda and suspend licensing — aquifer recharge zone (MPF: 98 springs; company: 3), an ammonium-sulphate leach 'experimental in Brazil', proximity to a hospital, an airport safety zone and INB's Caldas uranium legacy — and asked for a strategic assessment of the whole Poços de Caldas plateau. Copam granted the Preliminary Licence anyway on 19 Dec 2025 after a favourable FEAM opinion; the MPF said its work continues, and Copam's own mandates had lapsed since May 2025. Installation Licence submitted 19 May 2026, not yet granted; Operating Licence and the Southern Complex approvals still ahead.

Federal money says yes, federal prosecutors say not yet — the Installation Licence is the test, and a court challenge to the LP is the tail risk.

Differentiator

The only ionic-clay rare-earth developer outside China with a DFS, a preliminary licence, a running demonstration plant and the equity committed — and the highest-grade Measured magnet-REO IAC resource by the company's claim. Refined MREC with 64% NdPr and the lanthanum stripped is the feedstock Solvay's La Rochelle wants; C1 US$9.84/kg REO puts it low on the cost curve, with no tailings dam and a zero-liquid-discharge design. Sovereign-flavoured money (Vale/BNDES fund managers, BNDES, OneIM) and three ECA letters give it a financing path that Western peers lack.

Counterweight: a merchant MREC seller with no binding buyer yet, a plant that is first-of-its-kind at this scale in Brazil, and economics quoted at a NdPr deck above today's price.

Exploration upside

Large resource, plan uses 40% of it · moves the needle: low–medium The reserve already runs 41 years at 5 Mtpa and the DFS values only 25; the Southern Complex (separate approvals) and the 168 Mt Inferred are outside the plan. Drilling adjacent to the resource is in the use of proceeds, but tonnes are not the constraint — permits, debt and price are. The second leg is more likely downstream: Viridion (50/50 with Ionic Rare Earths) is planning a Brazilian separation/magnet-recycling centre (CRITR) with a FINEP application of up to R$40M, no economics published.

Next Catalyst

Lender term sheets and credit approvals (company target end-Sep 2026, not announced as of 15 Oct) and the binding Solvay offtake (target Q3 2026, not announced) are the two overdue items; then the September quarterly (late Oct), the AGM on 4 Nov (OneIM tranche 2, performance rights), the Installation Licence from FEAM, and FID / financial close in Q4 2026. Grid energisation Dec 2027, first production H2 2028.

Open Questions
  • Where are the lender term sheets? The end-September target passed without an announcement — is the ~US$280M debt still 70% of capex, and which ECAs are in?
  • Will Solvay sign a binding offtake with a price mechanism or floor, and for what share of MREC? The LoI (2 Jun 2026) has no volumes or price.
  • When does FEAM grant the Installation Licence, and has the MPF filed or threatened a civil action against the Preliminary Licence?
  • What are the spot Dy and Tb prices in the DFS spot case, and how much of the NPV is Dy/Tb at Western floors versus China prices?
  • Why did TREO output halve from the PFS (9,448 t → 4,446 t) while capex rose 25% — is the refined-MREC circuit a buyer requirement or a margin choice?
  • What is the Independent Technical Report's view (targeted end-Aug 2026, not published)?
  • What price will ORE/Régia tranches 3–4 be struck at, and does OneIM tranche 2 close at A$3.70 or A$4.50?
  • Has the demonstration plant run continuously since July at ≥76% MREO recovery on Northern Concessions ore, including reagent recovery and residue filtration?
Context & updates
10 Oct 2026 update

Weekly review: a Metrópoles report (13 Sep) says federal prosecutors asked the Federal Court, inside a civil action filed by local groups, to suspend Colossus licensing, including the Installation Licence decision, until the nuclear authority ANSN gives a final radioactivity opinion. Only one outlet so far and no ruling, so it stays unconfirmed and the odds are unchanged. If a court grants it, the Installation Licence and the Q4 2026 FID would slip. The 9 Oct Appendix 3B is director performance rights for the AGM, not a raise.

  • 20 AugVMM Secures Up to US$120M Strategic Equity FundingMatters most: OneIM US$75M in two tranches (T1 A$61.3M at A$3.79 done), ~US$40M from Brazilian institutions at the same price, ORE/Régia US$5M at A$3.38 accelerated; equity requirement US$135M covered on paper. Term sheets end-Sep, FID Q4 2026.

Links go to the company's own site · all releases · grey notes are my read of whether a release changes this card.

Management review
Bsolid, or unproven but clean (the default)Track record 3/5 · Fit 3/5 · Candour 3/5 · Skin in the game 3/5

Fast, competent delivery of studies, licence and equity by a team that has not built a mine; dates slip in small steps and the two most recent targets passed in silence; pay is rising ahead of delivery. Solid but unproven at the job that starts now.

Effect on the odds: none — neutral by default; odds move only with evidence.

People
Rafael Moreno (Managing Director & CEO, since 15 Jan 2024): Chemical engineer (Curtin). Ex-COO Argosy Minerals (Rincon lithium, Argentina, May 2022–Jan 2024); earlier senior project roles on Santos Barossa and INPEX Ichthys (LNG) and Rincon; budgets 'over US$2B'. Brazil-based. FY26 pay ~A$1.18M (42% salary, 58% bonus/equity); 2.25M performance rights (~A$7.35M at A$3.57) proposed at the 4 Nov 2026 AGM. Holds 1.14M shares (0.71%). · Agha Shahzad Pervez (Executive Chairman, since Jan 2021): Perth corporate/CFO background (Battery Age Minerals, Resonance Health). Also Non-Exec Chair of Bayan Mining & Minerals, Eminence Minerals and Pioneer Minerals — three other ASX micro-caps. FY26 pay ~A$494k; 750k performance rights proposed. Holds 5.79M shares (3.6%): placement at A$0.91 (Dec 2025, A$250k) and 200k options exercised at A$3.68 (Jul 2026, A$736k). · Geoffrey Bedford (Non-Executive Director, since 1 May 2026): Described by the company as a 'global rare-earth leader' (ex-Neo Performance Materials background, not verified here). 150k performance rights; no shares. · Marcus Silberman (Non-Executive Director, since 2 Feb 2026): US investment banker (Credit Suisse, Bank of America; 'US$140B of transactions'); Cemig board. Bought 5,000 shares at A$3.56 (Jun 2026); 100k performance rights. · Jose Carlos Guedes Rosado (Non-Executive Director, since ~early 2024): Brazilian director; 0.16% holding; up for re-election 4 Nov 2026. · Ross Forzatti (COO, since 3 Feb 2026): 24 years in rare-earth and battery-materials projects; set up Hatch's pyrometallurgy practice and led the Colossus scoping and PFS at Hatch before joining — the one builder on the team. · Ramon Soares / Marcia Cota (CFO / Head of Finance, since 2026): New in role (<1 year, Simply Wall St).
Track record
  • Moreno: COO of Argosy Minerals during the Rincon 2,000 tpa lithium-carbonate plant build and commissioning (2022–23); Argosy's plant ran well below nameplate and the company later struggled — a small, troubled build, not a mine delivered (https://businessnews.com.au/Person/Rafael-Moreno; outcome at Argosy not verified in detail)
  • Viridis under Moreno: scoping study (early 2025) → PFS 9 Jul 2025 → Preliminary Licence 19 Dec 2025 → first MREC from the demo plant May 2026 → DFS 20 Aug 2026 → US$120M equity 20 Aug 2026 — about 2.5 years from first resource to DFS, fast for the sectorsource
  • Capex and output drift: PFS US$358M for 9,448 t TREO/yr → DFS US$449M for 4,446 t TREO/yr (refined MREC, lanthanum removed); the headline NPV rose only because the NdPr deck moved from US$90 to US$129source
  • Pervez: chairs three other ASX micro-caps (Bayan, Eminence, Pioneer) — a corporate-shell background rather than a mine-building onesource
  • Board refresh in 2026: Faheem Ahmed out (Feb), Tim Harrison (Ionic Rare Earths) out (1 May), Silberman and Bedford in — moving from explorer-era directors to finance and rare-earth industry namessource

Boards today: Pervez: Executive Chair of Viridis plus Non-Exec Chair of Bayan Mining & Minerals, Eminence Minerals and Pioneer Minerals (four ASX roles). Moreno: Viridis only (as far as found). Silberman: Cemig (Brazil). Bedford and Rosado: not checked.

Fit to this job
Stage: developer moving to financing and construction — fits Forzatti (built the studies at Hatch) and Silberman (project debt, Brazil); Moreno is an LNG/lithium project-execution engineer who has run one small commissioning, not a mine CEO who has delivered a plant. Deposit/process: ionic-clay ammonium-sulphate leach, new to Brazil — nobody on the board has operated one; Bedford brings downstream rare-earth market knowledge. Region: Brazil — yes, Moreno is based there, Rosado and Silberman are Brazilian-connected, and the shareholder base is now Brazilian funds. New for them: raising and spending US$450M.
Skin & pay
3/5 — Chair holds 3.6% bought with cash in placements and an option exercise; CEO under 1%; no director selling found. Pervez 5.79M shares (3.6%, ~A$22M at A$3.83) — bought with cash in placements (A$0.91 Dec 2025) and an option exercise at A$3.68 (Jul 2026, A$736k); no sales found. Moreno 1.14M shares (0.71%, ~A$4.4M), origin not verified (likely rights/placement). Silberman 5,000 shares bought at A$3.56. Rosado 0.16%. Bedford nil. No open-market selling by directors found in 2026. Sources: https://www.marketindex.com.au/asx/directors/agha-pervez ; https://simplywall.st/stocks/au/materials/chia-vmm/viridis-mining-and-minerals-shares/ownership

Skin in the game is a signal of the team's own conviction: it never moves the odds, but breaks near-ties in list B of the rankings.

Red flags
  • Two dated promises (lender term sheets end-Sep, binding Solvay Q3) have passed without any company update — the pattern to watch going into FID.
  • Performance rights worth ~A$10.8M proposed at the AGM (Moreno ~A$7.35M) on top of A$1.18M FY26 pay, for a CEO holding 0.71%; hurdles are share-price VWAPs of A$5.50–7.50 plus milestones.
  • Executive Chairman chairs three other ASX micro-caps; combined executive-chair model with a CEO.
  • Governance challenge at the AGM: Stephen Mayne nominated over a physical-only meeting and no retail SPP alongside A$118M of placements to institutions; board recommends against.
  • Headline NPV depends on a single-day NdPr print (US$129) well above today's China price; the company's 'Western floor' case leans on Dy/Tb floor prices that only MP/Serra Verde have contractually.
  • Finance team (CFO, head of finance) all under a year in role entering a US$450M project financing.

What the numbers say
  1. Cash went from A$5.2M (30 Jun 2024) to a low of A$1.1M at 30 Jun 2025 (0.69 quarters of funding in the June-2025 Appendix 5B, 1.27 in March 2025) and back to A$20.5M at 30 Jun 2026; the June-2026 quarter outflow was A$9.6M (A$1.3M operating + A$5.1M E&E + A$3.2M PP&E for the demonstration plant), i.e. 3.2 quarters per the 5B, before the August-2026 A$117M placement (30.9M shares at A$3.79, OneIM A$61.3M + ~US$40M Brazilian/strategic investors) that takes pro forma cash to roughly A$135M plus US$25M undrawn from ORE/Regia and up to A$44.8M from OneIM tranche 2 (AGM resolution 14).
  2. Over FY25-FY26 the Appendix 5Bs show A$7.2M of administration/corporate payments against A$22.3M into E&E, A$2.2M tenements/mining rights and A$4.6M PP&E, so overhead is about 20% of cash spend; but overhead is rising fast with the build-up (A$0.5-0.7M a quarter in FY25, A$1.2-1.5M a quarter in H2 FY26) and KMP remuneration doubled to A$2.0M in FY26 (MD A$1.18M, executive chairman A$0.49M on a consultancy of A$54k p.a. + A$1,500/day).
  3. Funding is 100% equity and heavily dilutive at low prices before the re-rating: A$4.0M at A$0.52 (Oct 2024), A$3.5M at A$0.33 (Feb 2025), A$11.5M at A$0.91 (Jul/Aug 2025), US$5M (A$7.6M) at A$0.91 from ORE Investments/Regia Capital (Dec 2025), A$25M at A$1.90 (Mar 2026), then A$117M at A$3.79 (Aug 2026); shares rose from about 63.9M (Jun 2024) to 86.2M (Jun 2025), 129.7M (Jun 2026) and 161.1M (Oct 2026), +152% in 27 months, while the share price went from A$0.47 (30 Jun 2025) to A$3.5-4.2.
  4. Net loss A$8.3M in FY24 (A$4.3M share-based payments + A$1.4M FX loss), A$2.7M in FY25 and A$6.9M in FY26 (H1 FY26 A$2.7M incl. a A$0.9M FX loss; the H2 jump is not itemised because the FY26 statements could not be read); losses are overhead, FX on BRL balances and share-based pay, since exploration and project costs are capitalised (E&E asset A$15.8M -> A$27.7M -> A$30.7M at Dec 2025; total assets A$78.8M and equity A$73.7M at 30 Jun 2026).
  5. Unusual items: no debt, but financing is being lined up for US$449M capex (BNDES R$77.5M 16-year facility approved 9 Sep 2026, EFA letter of support US$50M, EDC LOI US$100M, Bpifrance LoS); a US$3.0M advance royalty plus 5.0M shares to the Colossus vendors (addendum Aug 2025, A$1.4M paid in the March-2026 quarter); shares suspended 13-20 Aug 2026 pending the DFS and the equity package; a non-board-endorsed director nomination (Stephen Mayne) at the 4 Nov 2026 AGM.

A Western rare-earth separator with government co-funding and a 2027 start date — but no binding feed, no binding sale and no published economics, at a valuation the funded phases of the plant don't support at today's NdPr price

Gate Machine A has to run continuously in Louisiana in H1 2027 on real commercial feed and make oxide that passes a named customer's qualification — the first proof that RapidSX works at tonnes-per-day scale and that Ucore can buy feed and sell product at a margin. Sub-gates: (1) factory-acceptance results for the scaled-up RapidSX assemblies (planned Aug–Sep 2026, not yet published) and Machine A commissioned in H1 2027 within US$60M; (2) a binding feed contract covering ≥600 tpa from 2027 at a payable that leaves a separation margin; (3) a binding offtake with a price mechanism, ideally a floor — ex-China NdPr is ~US$95/kg and the US$110 floor is MP's alone; (4) ~US$70M for Production Line 1 and the oxide block, above C$2.80 or from non-dilutive money; (5) China's heavy-REE controls staying in force past the suspension, now extended to 10 Jan 2027 so the Dy/Tb/Sm/Gd premium persists.

Thesis

Ucore is a pre-commercial, merchant rare-earth separator. It owns RapidSX, a compact column-based solvent-extraction platform (conventional SX chemistry in faster, smaller contactors — claimed up to 60% smaller footprint), proven on a 52-stage demo plant in Kingston, Ontario: 5,726 operating hours and ~1.8 t of REO-equivalent processed over seven separations to Jan 2026, 7,400+ hours of simulated runtime, 99.5% NdPr from two feed types and 99.9% Dy with a conventional polish. It is building the Louisiana Strategic Metals Complex in a brownfield building at England Airpark, Alexandria. A May 2026 engineering re-base (Orbital) set the plan as Machine A (≈600 tpa TREO, US$60M, commissioning H1 2027) then Production Line 1 (≈3,000 tpa NdPr, US$44M + a US$31M oxide/packaging block, undated, unfunded), ≈9,600 tpa at full build. Capex for the first 3,600 tpa doubled from US$65M to US$135M while first-phase capacity shrank from 2,000 tpa (2026) to 600 tpa (2027).

Funding: War Department awards US$27.0M (only US$1.9M of Phase 2 claimed by 30 Jun), Canadian C$4.28M plus a conditional C$36.3M, up to US$15M of Louisiana incentives; C$69M raised at C$2.80 in August, director loans repaid in September — est. ~C$75M cash, no debt, 152.5M shares (161M fully diluted, +31% in four months). Commercially nothing is binding: 0% of capacity contracted for feed or for sales.

At C$2.59 (US$278M; EV ~US$226M) the market pays US$63k per tpa of costed capacity against a US$37.5k/tpa build cost, and on my illustrative merchant-separator assumptions the 3,600 tpa build earns ~US$12M EBITDA at today's ex-China NdPr — the market cap is 15–40× that. The bull case is heavy-REE scarcity (Dy/Tb at ~5× China while the April-2025 controls hold) plus a US-policy champion premium or a strategic deal; the bear case is a technology/feed/offtake triple unknown, a two-year slip history and plant economics that don't cover the valuation. Stock −53% YTD, −80% from the C$13.07 high of late 2025, below the August deal price.

The Gate

Machine A has to run continuously in Louisiana in H1 2027 on real commercial feed and make oxide that passes a named customer's qualification — the first proof that RapidSX works at tonnes-per-day scale and that Ucore can buy feed and sell product at a margin.

Everything that matters for a processor is still open at once: whether the technology scales, where the feed comes from, who buys the product at what price, and how the second phase is paid for. Machine A answers the first and forces answers to the next two; until then the share price trades on China headlines and US funding news.

Sub-gates
  1. Scale-up: RSX-2/RSX-3 factory acceptance tests (Aug–Sep 2026) — results not released as of 2 Oct; Machine A installation, testing and commissioning H1 2027 (was 'early 2025' in 2023, then H2 2026, then mid-2026).
  2. Feed: eight-plus MOUs, LOIs and heads of agreement (thyssenkrupp, Meteoric, ABx, Critical Metals/Tanbreez, Hastings/Wyloo, Defense Metals, Cyclic, Sumitomo) — none binding, most suppliers themselves pre-production; the company conditions 2027 production on 'availability of commercial feedstock'.
  3. Offtake: MOUs with VAC/eVAC (nine-month window for definitive terms lapsed ~Aug 2026), Vulcan Elements (supply from 2027) and Sumitomo — no volumes, no price, no floor.
  4. Line 1 funding: ~US$70M gap (US$60–90M with feed working capital) ≈ +25% shares at C$2.59; no EXIM, DOE or DPA application disclosed.
  5. Policy: China's suspension of the October-2025 controls expires 10 Nov 2026 (an extension to 10 Jan 2027 under the 28 Sep US–China truce is not confirmed); the April-2025 heavy-REE licensing is what keeps Western Dy/Tb at ~5× China.
Product, price & policy lever

Product & processing. Separated rare-earth oxides: NdPr oxide (the volume product), plus Dy, Tb, Sm and Gd from heavy feeds (the value products), from a merchant separation plant that buys mixed concentrate (MREC/MREO) from third parties. Kingston demo output so far: 99.5% NdPr from ~2 t of Vietnamese ionic-clay MREO and ~5 t of North American bastnaesite MREC (Jun–Aug 2026), and 99.9% Dy, which needed a conventional solvent-extraction polish after the RapidSX circuit (Jul 2026). Samples have gone to unnamed Western, Japanese and Korean magnet makers; nothing has passed qualification, and formal qualification trials are scheduled for H1 2027 on Machine A output. Not yet proven: continuous commercial-scale RapidSX (Machine A ≈600 tpa is roughly 25–30× the demo's implied annual rate) and the US$31M oxide production and packaging block, which is not built.

Price. Opaque and split by geography. Ex-China NdPr oxide ~US$95/kg CIF (Benchmark, 21 Aug 2026), below China's domestic ~US$107.5/kg: the Western NdPr premium has inverted on ex-China oversupply, and is ~30% off the late-April peak. The heavies are the opposite: Dy ~US$205/kg in China vs US$1,100–2,000+ ex-China, Tb ~US$990 vs US$3,600–5,000+ (REEx, Aug 2026), because China's April-2025 licensing on Sm, Gd, Tb and Dy is still in force. Prices are assessed by price-reporting agencies (Benchmark, Fastmarkets, SMM) and heavy-REE trade is bilateral and confidential. The US$110/kg NdPr floor applies to MP Materials only; Serra Verde–USA Rare Earth's floors (Dy US$575, Tb US$2,050) show what a Western heavy-REE contract can look like. S&P puts long-run NdPr cost at US$75–80/kg.

Policy lever. The case is a policy trade. US Department of War (formerly DoD) other-transaction awards total US$27.0M: Phase 1 US$4.0M, Phase 2 US$18.4M (Jun 2025) plus a US$4.6M Sm/Gd top-up (14 Sep 2026); only US$1.9M of Phase 2 had been claimed by 30 Jun, with 15 of 20 milestones outstanding. DPAS priority rating (Sep 2025). Canada: C$4.28M NRCan contribution plus a conditional C$36.3M package for a Kingston Sm/Gd plant, no definitive agreement yet. Louisiana: up to US$15M of mostly tax- and performance-based incentives. No DOE loan, EXIM letter or DPA Title III equity, unlike Aclara (EXIM LOI up to US$750M, Sep 2026) or MP (DoD equity, loan and floor, Jul 2025). China: the April-2025 heavy-REE licensing holds; the suspension of the October-2025 expanded controls expires 10 Nov 2026; the 28 Sep US–China truce extension to 10 Jan 2027 does not explicitly cover it (not confirmed as of 16 Oct). Durability: the award money is milestone-based and small against US$135M of capex, and the heavy-REE premium Ucore targets exists only while China's controls bite. A US–China settlement is the main policy downside.

Valuation Anchor

Processor anchor: EV per tpa of capacity, contracted share, margin per kg. EV ~US$226M = US$63k per tpa of the costed 3,600 tpa build, 1.7× what it costs to build; contracted share 0%; margin per kg not guided (illustratively US$2–5/kg TREO). On those numbers there is no starting discount — the market is paying for technology, policy and strategic optionality, not for plant cash flow.

What would change that: (1) feed bought at ≤60% payable, or (2) a sales contract with a floor like MP's or Serra Verde's — each worth US$5–10M of EBITDA at today's price; (3) China's heavy-REE controls hardening after 10 Nov, which lifts the Dy/Tb credits Machine A is configured for; (4) a government or strategic transaction that values RapidSX as a platform rather than one plant.

Two lenses on the upside. The numbers above are a merchant model. If separation is the chokepoint, a working separator keeps more of the value: on heavy-rich clay feed (illustratively ~4% Dy, ~0.7% Tb — a basket of ~US$85–90/kg TREO at Western prices vs ~US$27 for light feed) bought at a 50% payable, plus Line 1 at 60%, illustrative EBITDA is ~US$33M (~0.8× at today's prices, ~2× at US$180 NdPr). And a working Western heavy-REE separator would be priced as a scarce strategic asset, not on EBITDA: ~3–5× if Machine A runs and an offtake is binding. My odds of that 3× holding: ~19% (Machine A + a binding contract by end-2027 ~30% after the management review × ~55–60% that the re-rate holds from C$2.16, plus luck).

For completeness: against Bokan's M&I (~24 kt TREO) the EV is ~US$9,300/t TREO — not meaningful, since Bokan is deferred to the late 2020s and not in the plan.

Bucket: processor / midstream — no resource to value; the plant, its feed and its contracts are the asset.

Shareholder structure
Management & board
Director Randy Johnson ~13.2% (~20.1M shares via Orca Holdings, derived after the Sep warrant exercise). Excluding him, board and management own <0.3%: chair & CEO Pat Ryan 209k, COO Mike Schrider ~55–70k after sales, CFO Peter Manuel ~71k (May 2026 circular / MarketScreener).
Insider activity (12 mo)
Debt-to-equity swap, and an operator selling. Johnson exercised 10.27M warrants for C$8.5M (~C$0.83) on 9 Sep — five days after Ucore repaid him C$8.7M of loans, so cash-neutral. COO Schrider, who runs the Louisiana build, sold ~75k shares at C$2.61–2.90 between 27 Aug and 3 Sep. No on-market insider buying found.
Strategic & prominent
No corporate equity holder. Sumitomo (framework), Vulcan Elements and VAC/eVAC (MOUs) hold no shares. Hondo Private Equity invested C$2M (Jan 2025). The US government funds capex but is not a shareholder.
Institutions / retail
Largely retail / unidentified (~0.3% identified institutional, MarketScreener). August deal via Red Cloud and B. Riley. Coverage: Raymond James (Outperform, initiated C$14.50 Oct 2025), B. Riley (Buy, Sep 2026); average target ~C$8.80, range C$2.95–12.00.

Read: A retail-momentum register with one large insider who converts debt into equity and an operating executive selling into weakness. Price is set by policy headlines — +973% in 2025, −53% in 2026 — not by institutions underwriting cash flow. Expect the stock to trade around the 10 Nov China deadline and US funding news more than around engineering milestones.

Jurisdiction risk

USA (Louisiana) — supportive at every layer. Industrial brownfield at a former air-force base, multi-decade lease, up to US$15M of state incentives, port access via New Orleans, ~100 jobs; air permitting not reported as an issue. Federal: War Department milestone funding, DPAS priority rating, the January 2026 Section 232 proclamation on processed critical minerals. Risks: Gulf Coast hurricanes; Aclara is building a competing separation plant at Port of Vinton, Louisiana, with its own feed and a larger US cheque.

Canada (Kingston, Ontario) hosts the demo plant and the planned Sm/Gd facility (NRCan and FedDev support, conditional). Alaska holds Bokan (no current permitting). HQ Bedford, Nova Scotia. The real jurisdiction risk is geopolitical, not local: a US–China rare-earth settlement after the 10 Nov 2026 expiry would shrink the premium the plant is built to capture.

Differentiator

One of very few ex-China heavy-rare-earth separation efforts with a dated plant, US defence co-funding and a feed-agnostic process.

  1. Government-backed technology: US$27M of War Department awards, a DPAS rating and Canadian federal support behind RapidSX.
  2. The scarce elements: Dy, Tb, Sm and Gd, where ex-China prices are ~5× China's — Sm/Gd for defence magnets is a niche few Western players target.
  3. Feed-agnostic: demonstrated on ionic-clay MREO and bastnaesite MREC, with MOUs across Australia, Brazil, Greenland, Canada and Thailand.
  4. Modular, low-capex brownfield next to US magnet makers (Vulcan, VAC/eVAC) that are themselves government-backed; clean balance sheet after September.

Counterweight: RapidSX is still SX chemistry — the edge is footprint and speed, not a new separation principle, and the Dy product needed a conventional finish. Four-plus years of MOUs have produced no binding feed or offtake, and VAC's window lapsed. First production slipped two years while capex doubled. A merchant separator earns only the separation margin, set by feed terms. Competitors with their own feed or bigger government cheques (MP, Lynas, Energy Fuels, Aclara) are ahead.

Exploration upside

Not the value driver · moves the needle: low. Bokan-Dotson Ridge (Alaska): April 2026 MRE of 4.17 Mt @ ~0.58% TREO M&I (~24 kt contained), 35–40% heavy rare earths — unusually heavy, but small; H1 2026 spend C$32.5k and development deferred to the late 2020s. The real optionality is RapidSX licensing or partner plants (Kingston Sm/Gd), with no disclosed economics.

Next Catalyst

Now: RSX-2/RSX-3 factory acceptance results (planned Aug–Sep, not yet released); Oct–Dec: Canada's C$36.3M definitive agreement; 10 Nov 2026: China's suspension of its October-2025 controls expires; late Nov: Q3 financials (cash, War Department claims); any time: first binding feed or offtake (Vulcan, VAC, Sumitomo, Hastings/Wyloo, Critical Metals); H1 2027: Machine A installation, commissioning and qualification trials; from Jul 2027: Tanbreez feed per LOI; 2027–28: Line 1 funding and build.

Open Questions
  • What did the RSX-2/RSX-3 factory acceptance tests show — throughput per contactor, purity, uptime?
  • Which feed will Machine A run in H1 2027, from whom, at what payable — and is any of it binding?
  • Does the US$31M oxide block have to be built before Machine A can sell oxide? If so, Machine A is not fully funded.
  • What per-kg opex and margin are in the techno-economic assessment the War Department accepted in April?
  • Why did VAC's nine-month window pass without definitive terms, and will Vulcan's 2027 supply be binding with a floor?
  • How will Line 1 (~US$70M) be funded — EXIM, DPA Title III, DOE, a strategic such as Sumitomo, or more equity at C$2–3?
  • Is any of the Vietnamese or Thai-processed feed China-linked in ownership or processing, which could disqualify product for defence end-users?
  • What happens to the heavy-REE premium if China extends or broadens licence relief after 10 Nov 2026?
Context & updates
3 Oct 2026 refresh

Nothing new from the company since 14 Sep: the RSX-2/RSX-3 factory-acceptance results are now about a month overdue, no Canada definitive agreement, no binding feed or offtake. Prices moved its way: ex-China NdPr averaged ~US$106.5/kg in September (from ~US$95), and Dy oxide in Europe ~US$3,500/kg, ~16× China. China's suspension of its Oct-2025 controls now runs to 10 Jan 2027 with the US–China truce (announced 23 Sep). [Corrected 16 Oct: not confirmed — the truce extension of 28 Sep does not explicitly cover the rare-earth suspension, so 10 Nov 2026 stays the expiry on this card.]

War Department top-up for Sm/Gd (14 Sep 2026)

+US$4.6M → Phase 2 US$23.0M, total US$27.0M. Only US$1.9M of Phase 2 claimed by 30 Jun (5 of 20 milestones). The company's own totals are inconsistent (US$20.4M in an August release vs US$22.4M in the MD&A before the top-up).

Balance sheet reset (Aug–Sep 2026)

C$69.0M bought deal at C$2.80 closed 13 Aug (24.6M shares). Orca loans (C$8.7M incl. interest) repaid 4 Sep; Johnson exercised 10.27M warrants for C$8.5M on 9 Sep. 152.5M shares, no debt.

Capex doubled, first phase shrank (28 May 2026)

Orbital report: US$135M for ≈3,600 tpa (was US$65M); four production lines cut to three; Machine A commissioning H1 2027 (Dec 2025 guidance: Machine #1 installed mid-2026). Stock −3% on the day.

NdPr Western premium inverted (Aug 2026)

Ex-China NdPr US$95/kg vs China US$107.5/kg (Benchmark, 21 Aug), below the MP-only US$110 floor; heavy-REE premia persist (Dy ~5×, Tb ~4–5×).

Stock below deal price (Sep–Oct 2026)

C$2.59–2.78 vs the C$2.80 deal and the C$13.07 52-week high; the COO sold ~75k shares 27 Aug–3 Sep.

Management review
Creal weaknesses: slipped dates, thin fit or thin candourTrack record 2/5 · Fit 2/5 · Candour 2/5 · Skin in the game 1/5

Nineteen years listed, three plant plans, none delivered; the Louisiana timeline was re-set again in May 2026 and framed as an upgrade. Real DoW backing noted.

Effect on the odds: chance it works 35% → 30%, odds of a 3× 18% → 16%.

People
Pat Ryan, P.Eng. (Chairman & CEO, since Board 2014; chair ~2015; CEO (initially interim) June 2020): Founder (1993) of Neocon International, automotive OEM parts design/manufacturing (private). No prior mining or chemical-processing company run. Owns ~132k shares (0.2%). · Mike Schrider, P.E. (VP & COO, since ~2020): Engineer; runs the Kingston demo plant and Louisiana build. The person responsible for the hardware delivering. · Peter Manuel (VP, CFO & Corporate Secretary, since long-standing (pre-2020)): Salary C$176k (2024). · Randy Johnson (Director; largest shareholder via Orca Holdings (14.4%), since unverified): Also lender to the company (Orca debt facilities, fully repaid Sep 2026 after the C$69M raise).
Track record
  • Company listed since 2007 as Bokan Mountain (Alaska) heavy-REE explorer; exploration stopped 2015 on price collapse; no mine builtsource
  • 2014: Alaska legislature authorised AIDEA to finance up to US$145M of Bokan; never drawn (same source)
  • 2018-19: planned US$25M Ketchikan separation plant 'up and running sometime in 2020' using IBC's MRT technology; ended in Utah litigation with IBC (2019-20); plant never builtsource
  • 2020: bought Innovation Metals (RapidSX); Jan 2021 'Alaska2023' plan for Alaska production in three years; abandoned for Louisiana in 2022-23source
  • 2025-26: real traction — US$18.4M + US$4M + US$4.6M US Department of War awards, Canadian support, Sumitomo collaboration (Jun 2026), NdPr and Dy oxide samples from demo plant, C$69M bought deal Aug 2026source
  • No commercial RapidSX plant has yet operated; capex for Machine A + first line raised to ~US$135Msource

Boards today: Ryan: Ucore (chair+CEO) + private Neocon = 1 public. Board of six, no new director in 3 years.

Fit to this job
Nobody on this team has built or run a commercial rare-earth separation plant (very few in the West have). The CEO's background is automotive supply chains, not hydrometallurgy; the technical case rests on COO Schrider and the Kingston demo data. Stage is new for them: moving from demo to a US$135M first line is the exact step they have announced and not completed for a decade, first in Alaska, now Louisiana. Government funding and offtake interest are the strongest external validation they have ever had.
Skin & pay
1/5 — under ~0.5%, options only, or net sellers while promoting. Insiders ~0.21% (~C$0.8M); CEO 132,290 shares as of May 2025 record date; Orca Holdings (director Randy Johnson) 14.4%. Insiders net sellers C$4.5M over 12 months to Sep 2026; a director exercised warrants Sep 2026. CEO total comp C$407.7k for 2025 (46% salary); 2024 base C$225k (CEO + chair fees) plus ~C$156k RSU/options — modest in absolute terms, now small relative to a ~C$500M+ market cap. Sources: https://ucore.com/Docs/Ucore_Circular_2025.pdf ; https://simplywall.st/stocks/ca/materials/tsxv-ucu/ucore-rare-metals-shares/management

Skin in the game is a signal of the team's own conviction: it never moves the odds, but breaks near-ties in list B of the rankings.

Red flags
  • Roughly a decade of announced plant start dates (2020 Ketchikan, 2023 Alaska, 2024-26 Louisiana) none met; the latest slip (2026 → 1H 2027) was presented as a strategy upgrade.
  • Litigation with former technology partner IBC (2019-20) over MRT.
  • CEO owns ~0.2%; insiders net sellers of ~C$4.5M in the last 12 months during a 7x share-price run.
  • Related-party lending: director Randy Johnson's Orca Holdings (14.4% holder) was the company's debt provider until Sep 2026.
  • Company website still shows the superseded 2026/2027/2028 tpa ramp.

What the numbers say
  1. Cash was C$19.9M at 30 June 2026, about 3.5 quarters at C$5.5M a quarter; the August C$69.0M raise lifts pro-forma cash to roughly C$85-90M.
  2. Money goes mainly into technology and the Louisiana plant: R&D C$6.5M in H1 2026 against cash overhead of C$2.7M, about 30-35% of spend.
  3. Shares rose from 61.8M in 2023 to 116.6M by June 2026 and about 151M after the August offering and warrant exercises.
  4. Government funding is material: US$22.4M awarded by the US Defense Department with US$16.5M still to claim, plus a new US$4.6M award in September 2026.
  5. The 2025 loss of C$39.3M included a C$21.7M non-cash convertible fair-value loss; all debentures converted and the Orca loans were repaid, so no debt remains.

ATHA Energy U₃O₈

TSXV: SASK · OTCQX: SASKF website
US$210MC$0.84 · 23 Oct 2026 (~C$295M)Risk 4/5
Rankings: A #4 · B #8 of 9 · 3×+ odds ~8%rankings ↗
Grade / discoveryFunded to catalyst2nd 3×: yesCash: ~17 monthsMgmt: C
No current resource, no study — historical 43.3 Mlb Inferred (2013) + a conceptual 60.8–98.2 Mlb target

Canada's largest uranium land-holder, funded to the end of 2027, with a Nunavut discovery corridor that hit in every 2025 hole — priced at ~US$2.70 per conceptual pound, which is a developer's price for an explorer whose 2026 results are still radiometric, not assayed

Gate Turning the RIB North and Lac 50 West radiometric hits into a compliant, assayed resource large enough (>100 Mlb at >0.3% U₃O₈) to justify a mine in Nunavut — proving the 'district' is more than wide low-grade envelopes with sporadic centimetre-scale high grade. Sub-gates: (1) Q4 2026 assays confirm counts-per-second translate to grade — multi-metre intervals above 0.5% U₃O₈, not just 0.5–1.5 m spikes; (2) 2027 delineation drilling at 75 × 75 m shows continuity along the 3.4 km mineralised trend; (3) the Lac 50 exploration target (60.8–98.2 Mlb) converted to a current MRE at ≥0.4%; (4) metallurgy and preliminary economics that survive remote-Nunavut capex and the Kiggavik social-licence precedent; (5) a term price that stays ≥US$80/lb so a 2030s Nunavut project is financeable.

Deposit Angilak: historical Lac 50 2.83 Mt @ 0.69% U₃O₈ = 43.3 Mlb Inferred (2013, not current) · exploration target 60.8–98.2 Mlb @ 0.37–0.48% (conceptual) · RIB North mineralised trend 3.4 km (21/23 holes, cps; ~300 m higher-grade sub-zone; assays pending)
Nunavut · Athabasca · LabradorExplorer (discovery · funded to end-2027)Insiders ~0.9% (CEO sold in 2026)

Uranium · 100% uranium — no resource, no study; Nunavut discovery corridor plus Athabasca and Labrador land · Reviewed Oct 2026

Funding & next catalyst
Cash
C$47.6M at 30 Jun 2026 (Q2 statements); ~C$34M est. at 30 Sep 2026; funded through 31 Dec 2027 per the company
Debt
US$25M 12% senior convertible debenture to Queen's Road Capital (Feb 2026, matures Feb 2031, converts at C$0.85 — in the money; 8% cash + 4% in shares); QRC holds an option for a further US$25M to Feb 2028. ~12% dilution on conversion
Burn · runway
~C$14M in the June–September quarter (three rigs, ~20,000 m); C$63M raised Feb 2026 (the debenture + C$28.75M flow-through at C$1.02, a 62% premium) guided to cover ~24 months. Flow-through must be spent on Canadian exploration
Next catalyst
Q4 2026 assays for RIBN-DD-010 to -016A and Lac 50 West → 3D EM inversions Q4 → Q3 financials late Nov (30 Jun cash C$47.6M) → 2027 program and MRE-pathway decision Jan–Feb → delineation drilling May–Sep 2027 → maiden/updated MRE 2027–28 (soft)
Funded to the next catalyst Funded for the 2026–27 exploration program with no gap to a dated catalyst — the best runway of the pre-resource uranium explorers. Not funded for a resource-to-PEA-to-permitting path in Nunavut (likely C$100M+ over five to eight years); expect equity at each stage, with the QRC conversion (~42M shares) already baked in.
Thesis

Built from the March 2024 three-way merger with Latitude Uranium (Angilak, Nunavut; the Central Mineral Belt, Labrador) and 92 Energy (the Gemini/GMZ discovery in the Athabasca Basin): 6.7–8.4M acres, the largest uranium land position in Canada. The flagship is Angilak (Kivalliq, Nunavut; 225 km south-west of Baker Lake; Lac 50 on Inuit Owned Land under an NTI exploration agreement): the historical Lac 50 deposit, 2.83 Mt @ 0.69% U₃O₈ for 43.3 Mlb Inferred (2013, Kivalliq Energy; not current), a 2024 conceptual target of 60.8–98.2 Mlb @ 0.37–0.48%, and the 2025–26 Mineralized RIB Corridor — 18 km, a 100% hit rate in 2025 (22 holes), a maiden RIB North hole of 34.7 m composite incl. 0.5 m @ 8.16% U₃O₈, and in 2026 twenty-three holes that traced the trend from 300 m to 3.4 km (RIBN-DD-003 37 m, -011 20 m, -023 19.5 m composite; 21 of 23 mineralised, with a ~300 m higher-grade sub-zone) plus a new Lac 50 West discovery 4 km from the deposit. 2026: three rigs, ~20,000 m, May–September; all 2026 intercepts are radiometric — assays due Q4.

Elsewhere: ~3M acres in the Athabasca (GMZ/Gemini: 43 m @ 0.62% incl. 6 m @ 2.17% from 2023; Pasfield earn-in to 60%; Spire/Horizon optioned out to Terra Uranium), 10% carried interests on parts of NexGen and IsoEnergy ground, ~250,000 acres in Labrador — no 2025–26 fieldwork found on either. Money: C$63M in Feb 2026 — a US$25M 12% QRC convertible at C$0.85 plus C$28.75M of flow-through at C$1.02 — funded to end-2027; cash C$47.6M at 30 Jun 2026 (~C$34M est. at 30 Sep). Uranium: spot ~US$89/lb, term a record ~US$96 (above spot).

CEO Troy Boisjoli (15 years at Cameco — McArthur River, Eagle Point; ex-Latitude CEO), VP Exploration Cliff Revering (ex-Cameco). Register ~21% uranium ETFs (URNM 14.2%, URA 6.7%), insiders ~0.9%, and the CEO sold ~1.02M shares in March and July. At C$0.93 (15 Oct) the market cap is ~C$329M / US$232M (FD ~C$415M): ~US$2.9 per conceptual pound, ~US$5.4 per historical Inferred pound — Nunavut explorers usually trade under US$2, Athabasca developers with resources above US$10.

The Gate

Turning the RIB North and Lac 50 West radiometric hits into a compliant, assayed resource large enough (>100 Mlb at >0.3% U₃O₈) to justify a mine in Nunavut — proving the 'district' is more than wide low-grade envelopes with sporadic centimetre-scale high grade.

The 2026 program delivered what an explorer promises — 23 holes, 21 mineralised, a trend traced from 300 m to 3.4 km with a ~300 m higher-grade sub-zone, a new discovery 4 km from the deposit — and every one of those intercepts is a scintillometer reading. 'Composite' intervals sum many thin zones across 100–200 m of core (RIBN-DD-011's 20 m sits between 494 and 700 m). The assays in Q4 are the first hard test; the resource is 2027–28; the mine, if ever, is a decade and a Nunavut review away.

Sub-gates
  1. Q4 2026 assays: the cps-to-grade test — need multi-metre intervals >0.5% U₃O₈ on the RIB North and Lac 50 West holes (SRC Saskatoon, 6–8 weeks).
  2. 2027 delineation: 75 × 75 m on the best zone (RIB North vs Lac 50 trade-off study in 2026) — continuity, not more strike.
  3. An MRE: the 2024 target (60.8–98.2 Mlb @ 0.37–0.48%) is conceptual; the 2013 43.3 Mlb is historical; nothing is current.
  4. Economics in Nunavut: fly-in, no road, Feb–Oct season, NIRB + Water Board + CNSC — and Kiggavik, rejected in 2016 near Baker Lake.
  5. Term ≥ US$80/lb through the resource stage; the register is ~21% uranium ETFs, so the stock follows flows as much as drill holes.
Product, price & policy lever

Product & processing. Would sell yellowcake (U₃O₈) to Western utilities and converters (Cameco's Blind River / Port Hope). Nothing downstream exists: no metallurgy for Lac 50 or RIB in current sources, no mill design, no tailings or environmental work, no NIRB or Water Board process started. Angilak is fly-in with a February–October season; a mine needs an all-weather or winter road and its own power. Earliest realistic production is beyond 2035 — the realistic exit is a takeout once a resource exists.

Price. Spot ~US$89/lb (29 Sep 2026, +20% on the year); long-term ~US$96–96.50 (10 Sep), a nominal record and above spot — the inversion that signals utilities moving toward replacement-rate contracting, though Cameco says they are not there yet. No exchange: daily broker indicators (UxC, TradeTech, Numerco) and monthly term indicators; producers' realised prices lag (Cameco ~US$67 in Q2). Supply: Kazatomprom's 2026 cut (~8 Mlb) and refusal to expand at any spot price; Cameco 19.5–21.5 Mlb reaffirmed. For a 2030s project the term curve, not spot, is the number — and it needs to hold US$80+ for a decade.

Policy lever. (1) The Western 'secure supply' premium — the US Prohibiting Russian Uranium Imports Act, with waivers expiring by 1 Jan 2028 while Russia still supplied ~26% of US enrichment in 2025 — tightens demand for Western-origin pounds into exactly the window a resource would be defined; bipartisan law, durable. (2) Canadian exploration tax policy — the C$28.75M flow-through at a 62% premium is taxpayer-subsidised drilling, already received. (3) Nunavut's stance on uranium — the fragile lever: NIRB rejected Kiggavik in 2015–16; NTI's 2007 policy allows uranium mining with conditions. No DoD/DPA/EXIM money and none expected for a Canadian explorer.
