Management review
Creal weaknesses: slipped dates, thin fit or thin candourTrack record 3/5 · Fit 3/5 · Candour 2/5 · Skin in the game 3/5

A twenty-year MD who built a small mine, shut it, and signed OEM and DoE deals that are real — but the refinery date has gone from 2026 to nothing, the Japan FID vanished, and construction awards slip while the commissioning date is repeated. Dates are not held and slips are not named.

Effect on the odds: chance it works 35% → 32%, odds of a 3× 11% → 10%.

People
Justin Brown (Managing Director, since May 2006 (20 years)): Geologist; has run the company from its lithium/manganese exploration days through Butcherbird Stage 1 (first concentrate 2021), the 2024 shutdown and the HPMSM strategy. ~3.0% holder, options exercised for cash in 2024–25. FY2025 pay A$459.8k (FY2024 A$424k, FY2023 A$671k). · John Ribbons (Non-Executive Chairman, since Jul 2010 (16 years)): Lawyer/corporate; ~2.4M shares (0.7%). Fees A$111.5k. · Salvatore (Sam) Lancuba (Non-Executive Director, since Jan 2023): Former Fortescue executive; 37k shares. Fees A$60.7k. · Fanie van Jaarsveld (Former NED (retired 1 Jan 2026), now consultant, since —): Bought 175,000 shares on market 4 Nov 2024; left the board at the start of 2026.
Track record
  • Brown built Butcherbird Stage 1 and shipped concentrate from 2021 (FY2024 revenue A$13.3M, FY2025 A$1.9M), then suspended production in early 2024 'during a period of depressed ore prices'source
  • Signed Stellantis (Jan 2023: US$15M equity at A$1.00 + US$15M prepayment, 45 kt take-or-pay) and GM (Jun 2023: US$85M loan, up to 32,500 tpa) for a Louisiana refinery with 'supply targeted to start in 2026' — no plant, no date today; GM's timing CPs lapsed (https://www.miningweekly.com/article/element-strikes-offtake-and-funding-deal-with-stellantis-2023-01-09; https://www.listcorp.com/asx/e25/element-25-limited/news/update-on-us-hpmsm-project-execution-plan-3357355.html)
  • Won a US$166M DoE Battery Materials Processing grant (selected Sep 2024, signed Jan 2025, revised terms Jul 2026) — a real, rare win for an ASX micro-cap (https://announcements.asx.com.au/asxpdf/20240924/pdf/06874p1n4ydt0p.pdf; https://kalkine.com.au/news/mining-materials/element-25-asxe25-what-the-revised-doe-grant-agreement-means-for-the-hpmsm-louisiana-project)
  • Closed NAIF A$50M (approved Jun 2025, contractual close Sep 2026) and a binding OM Materials take-or-pay offtake (Aug 2026) for the expansion (https://bulletin.webull.com/qbd/announcement/20260908/499502054/8f91a8d12680db7efaa5c6849d0b6acc.pdf; https://www.miningweekly.com/article/element25-subsidiary-signs-manganese-offtake-deal-with-om-materials-2026-08-03)
  • Share price A$2.90 (Mar 2021) → A$0.23; Stellantis's A$1.00 entry is 77% under water; raises at A$0.33, A$0.28 and A$0.30 in 2025–26source

Boards today: Brown: Element 25 only (no other listed boards found). Ribbons: chair for 16 years; Lancuba: one NED. A three-person board with no independent mining-build experience listed and no new director in three years (Simply Wall St note).

Fit to this job
Stage: a mine restart and a 1.1 Mtpa process-plant build — Brown has built and run the smaller Stage 1, so the mining side fits. The HPMSM refinery is a chemical plant in the US with OEM counterparties and federal grant compliance; nobody on the board has built one, and the company relies on contractors (Altris as lead engineer, Veolia partnership). Region: Pilbara, yes; Louisiana, no in-house experience.
Skin & pay
3/5 — meaningful stake (~3%), partly bought by exercising options for cash; no selling; pay modest against the cap. Brown ~9.8M shares ≈ 3.0% (~A$2.3M at A$0.23) after converting 502,872 performance rights on 30 Sep 2026; 1.0M options (A$1.58 and A$0.67, out of the money) and ~1.0M performance rights. Exercised options for cash at ~A$0.20–0.35 in Nov 2024 and Nov 2025 (~A$0.2–0.3M each) and bought 111,112 shares on market with Ribbons on 4 Jul 2024; no sales found. Pay A$459.8k (FY2025) ≈ 0.6% of market cap — modest. Skin in the game is a signal of the team's own conviction: it never moves the odds, but breaks near-ties in list B of the rankings.

Red flags
  • The flagship HPMSM project has slipped from 'supply in 2026' to no date, and the release that said so came after market with no figures.
  • Mine shut once on price (early 2024) — the same price exposure applies to the 1.1 Mtpa restart, which produces 31.6% Mn lump below the benchmark grades.
  • Japan FID target (June 2026) dropped silently.
  • Construction contract still unawarded a quarter after its target while 'Q1 2027 commissioning' is repeated unchanged.
  • Serial small raises at falling prices (A$1.00 → A$0.33 → A$0.28 → A$0.30) plus an ATM; MD pay (A$460k) rose in FY2025 while the stock fell.

What the numbers say
  1. The mine does not cover corporate costs: Butcherbird has been on care and maintenance since early 2024, revenue fell from A$13.3M in FY24 (cost of sales A$30.4M, gross loss A$17.1M) to A$1.9M in FY25 (one last stockpile sale, A$1.78M received in the June-2025 quarter, against A$5.4M cost of sales) and to nil in FY26; operating cash flow was -A$20.0M, -A$4.8M and -A$2.8M, so the company has been living on equity while it builds the 1.1Mtpa expansion (BBX, A$64.8M capex per the Jan-2025 feasibility study, commissioning targeted Q1 CY2027).
  2. Cash went from A$11.3M (30 Jun 2024) to A$2.2M (30 Jun 2025, only 1.9 quarters of funding at the time) and back to A$18.3M at 30 Jun 2026 after a A$10M placement at A$0.28 (Sep 2025) and a A$18M placement at A$0.30 (Apr 2026); shares rose 48% from 219.5M to 325.8M in two years, plus A$2.9M drawn from the Acuity Capital ATM (8.7M shares at A$0.3275, Mar 2025). No borrowings yet: the A$50M NAIF senior debt (A$42.5M term + A$7.5M cost overrun) reached contractual close on 8 Sep 2026 but first drawdown is still subject to conditions.
  3. Where the money went in FY25-FY26: about A$15.7M of PP&E payments for the BBX expansion (sizers, logwasher, engineering, deposits), A$1.2M of tenement/E&E payments, and roughly A$6.0M of staff and administration (A$0.6-1.7M a quarter, A$1.4M in the June-2026 quarter), so overhead is around a quarter of cash spend and shrinking relative to capex; G&A in the P&L fell from A$4.7M (FY24) to A$3.2M (FY25) and A$1.7M (FY26). Net loss narrowed from A$21.5M to A$5.9M to A$2.1M, the FY26 loss cushioned by A$1.2M other income (R&D refund A$0.78M, interest) but hit by a A$0.96M inventory write-down and A$0.67M care-and-maintenance cost.
  4. Unusual: the June-2026 Appendix 5B starts the quarter from the December-2025 cash balance (A$7.77M) instead of the March-2026 balance (A$4.36M), and both its 12-month column and the audited FY26 cash-flow statement omit the March-quarter outflows (A$1.7M operating, A$1.7M investing); the audited closing cash of A$18.28M is consistent with the June 5B, so either the March 5B overstated spending or the annual accounts under-state it by A$3.4M. Treat the quarterly burn rate with caution until the September-2026 quarterly is out.
  5. Downstream: the US$166M US DoE grant for the Louisiana HPMSM refinery (US$289M build, US$85M GM debt + US$30M Stellantis equity/prepayment committed) was re-papered in July 2026 with revised milestones, and the project execution plan is under review after the EV-market slowdown; no cash has been spent on it through the 5B 'HPMSM development' line in these eight quarters. Binding take-or-pay offtake for the whole BBX output was signed with OM Materials (OM Holdings) on 3 Aug 2026 and site works started 15 Sep 2026.

Caveats (11)Quarterly cash chain breaks between Q3 and Q4 FY26: the March-2026 Appendix 5B (29 Apr 2026) reports closing cash of A$4,357k after A$1,665k operating and A$1,732k investing outflows, but the June-2026 Appendix 5B (28 Jul 2026) opens the quarter at A$7,774k (the 31 Dec 2025 balance), its 12-month column equals the December YTD plus the June quarter, and the PwC-audited FY26 cash-flow statement (operating -2.81M, investing -7.65M, financing +26.62M, closing A$18,277,404) agrees with the June 5B, not with the sum of the four 5Bs (operating -4.5M, investing -9.4M). Either the March 5B is wrong or A$3.4M of March-quarter outflows are missing from the annual accounts. Both sets of numbers are kept as reported; the Q4 'check' records the gap.Sum-of-quarters vs FY26 statutory: cfo quarters -4.5 vs -2.8; capex+exp_cap quarters -9.0 vs -8.1; fin quarters 26.8 vs 26.8 (ok); net quarters +12.7 vs +16.1 (the same A$3.4M gap). FY25: cfo quarters -3.3 vs statutory -4.8 and investing quarters -9.1 vs -7.6 because the audited statement reclassified about A$1.4M of 5B investing payments to operating; fin 3.5 vs 3.5 and net -9.1 vs -9.2 (ok). Net-loss quarter checks are not possible (no quarterly income statements); H1 losses were A$2.4M (FY25) and A$1.9M (FY26).FY26 'exp_cap'/'capex' split (0.5 / 7.6) and the FY25 split (0.7 / 6.9) are imposed from the Appendix 5B tenement lines; the statutory statements show a single 'payments for plant and equipment' line (A$8.09M FY26, A$7.63M FY25).June-2026 5B investing lines were mislabelled in the mirror's text layer (A$287k shown against 'entities', A$5,180k against 'tenements', A$15k against 'PP&E'); they were re-assigned as tenements 287 / PP&E 5,180 / E&E 15 because the 12-month totals (464 / 7,603 / 24) then reconcile to the December YTD (177 / 2,423 / 9) plus the quarter, and to the statutory A$8.09M.Shares at 31 Mar 2026 (267.8M) and 30 Sep 2025 (266.9M) are carried from the nearest statutory count; small performance-rights conversions and cancellations (Appendix 2A/3H of 25 Mar, 7 Apr, 10 Apr 2026) are not itemised. 30 Jun 2026 count (325,810,579) is derived from the 14 Jul 2026 Appendix 2A (325,953,167 after 142,588 rights shares); the FY26 annual report's share-capital note was not in the readable extract. Count at 5 Oct 2026: 327,323,607 plus 5.45M unlisted options and 4.72M performance rights.The 9.5M shares placed with Acuity Capital as ATM security (nil consideration) are mentioned in the Dec-2025 quarterly but not in the half-year share-movement table; their treatment in the counts above is unknown (they may have been issued and later cancelled, e.g. the 10 Apr 2026 cessation notice).H1 FY25 'overhead' (2.4) is an allocation: 5B staff+admin for the two quarters is A$2.0M and site care-and-maintenance payments A$0.93M, but statutory payments to suppliers and employees were A$3.02M in total; FY25 overhead (5.3) includes the A$2.49M production/C&M payments because the statutory statement does not separate them.The FY24 annual report (27 Sep 2024) and the FY24/FY25 share-capital notes were not fetched directly; FY24 uses the audited comparative column of the FY25 report. FY24 'spend.overhead' is the whole operating outflow (A$20.0M) because mine operating costs and corporate costs are not separable in the statutory statement.NAIF: A$50M (A$42.5M senior term + A$7.5M cost-overrun) reached 'contractual close' on 8 Sep 2026 per the ASX headline; the announcement body (4 pages) could not be read, so conditions precedent and pricing are not recorded. The 5B facilities table shows nil at every quarter end through 30 Jun 2026.Market Index's quote page showed a 'HALTED SUSPENDED' badge for E25 on 10 Oct 2026 (as it did for other ASX small caps checked the same day); no trading halt or suspension notice after the 9 Apr 2026 placement halt appears in the ASX announcement index to 6 Oct 2026, so this is treated as a data artefact.All PDFs were read through mirrors (Market Index inline PDF, financialfilings.com / cdn.financialreports.eu); the ASX idsId of each original is given in asx_id. The company website document lists could not be read.

Appendix 5B figures are in A$'000, statutory statements in whole A$; everything here is A$ millions, one decimal. Quarters are the discrete three months read from each Appendix 5B (quarter column); income-statement lines are null for quarters because E25 publishes income statements only half-yearly and annually. H1 and FY rows use the statutory statements. 'net' is the change in cash including FX. 'cos' = cost of sales; in FY26/H1 FY26 the company shows 'care and maintenance costs' in that position and it is recorded there. 'ground' = tenement payments + capitalised/expensed exploration (small: Butcherbird is a development/capex story, not an exploration one). 'overhead' = 5B staff + administration payments (plus site production/care-and-maintenance payments where stated in the note); 'capex' = PP&E payments for the BBX expansion. The statutory cash-flow statement lumps tenement and E&E payments into 'payments for plant and equipment' and classifies about A$1.4M of FY25 5B investing payments as operating, so FY rows differ slightly from the sum of the 5Bs.

No resource, no metallurgy, no study — an exploration target only (2.7 Mt @ 3–4% Sb, Nov 2025)

Top-decile antimony grades over narrow widths in a Tier-1 jurisdiction — and nothing else yet: no resource (the maiden MRE is two quarters late), no metallurgy, under a year of cash, and an antimony price that has already given back two-thirds of its spike

Gate A maiden NI 43-101 resource that classifies a meaningful part of the 2.7 Mt @ 3–4% Sb exploration target — say ≥1.5 Mt at ≥3% Sb — the event the stock has been waiting for since Q1 2026. Sub-gates: (1) the MRE delivered by Q1 2027 with Main Zone continuity holding at 3–5 m widths — nuggety massive-stibnite pods are the classification risk; (2) first bench-scale metallurgy showing a clean gravity/flotation stibnite concentrate (≥50% Sb) with the gold recoverable or at least payable; (3) ≥C$5M of hard-dollar money in Q4 2026/Q1 2027 without a sub-C$0.40 price (the C$5M announced 29 Sep is flow-through — exploration spend only); (4) China's suspension of its US export ban lapsing on 27 Nov 2026, or any US/Canadian procurement or funding signal that re-rates Western antimony; (5) the NB environmental registration and Indigenous engagement plan progressing so the permit-application target doesn't slip into the price trough.

Not funded to the next catalyst Funded for the next drill season only (the flow-through closed 5 Oct) — and FT money can only pay for Canadian exploration. The MRE (C$0.2–0.5M), metallurgy (C$0.5–1M), environmental baseline and EIA (C$2–4M over two years), a PEA and ~C$2M/yr of G&A need C$8–12M of hard dollars over eighteen months on a ~C$54M market cap: 20–30% more dilution before any economic study exists.
Thesis

A pre-resource, single-asset antimony explorer. Bald Hill (Queens County, southern New Brunswick; highway access, Saint John port 55 km) is held under an option from Globex Mining (Jan 2025: C$2.0M cash over four years, 1.1M shares, C$5.0M of work; Globex keeps a 3.5% GMR) — the company calls it '100% owned', which it is not yet. What exists: a Nov 2025 technical report with an exploration target of 2.71 Mt @ 3–4% Sb (81–108 kt Sb) over a 600–700 m × 350–400 m Main Zone, 77 holes / 25,000 m to May 2026 plus a 19,000 m 2026 program on three rigs, spectacular narrow intercepts (27–36% Sb over 0.3–1.7 m inside 3–11 m zones of 2–4%; BH-26-21: 9.12 m @ 2.28% Sb incl. 0.3 m @ 27.3%, 11 Sep 2026), by-product gold of 0.2–1.9 g/t and one visible-gold spot read, a 594 line-km airborne survey (Aug), environmental baseline and an SRK gap analysis started in April, and a West Point critical-minerals advisor hired to open DoD/EXIM doors. Three further zones (Central, South, Marcus) and soil anomalies on the Second Run claims 3 km south.

What does not exist: a resource — the report targeted a maiden MRE by Q1 2026, commentary said July, and as of 29 Sep the company still cites only the conceptual 2.7 Mt; any metallurgy; any study; any offtake; any government money. Cash C$4.86M at 31 May against ~C$1.1M a month; a C$5M flow-through at C$0.47 announced 29 Sep, 15% above the market. Antimony has fallen ~67% from the Jul 2025 Rotterdam peak to ~US$19,800/t; China's suspension of its US export ban expires 27 Nov 2026; Perpetua's US$2.9B EXIM loan shows where Western antimony money goes — to a permitted US mine with gold.

CEO Jim Atkinson (76; head geologist at NB's Lake George antimony mine; also the company's only QP), outsourced CFO, a three-person board. At C$0.30 (15 Oct) the market cap is C$39.9M / US$28M, −82% from the March high: ~US$220–300 per 'target' tonne of antimony — a number against a denominator that is not yet classified.

The Gate

A maiden NI 43-101 resource that classifies a meaningful part of the 2.7 Mt @ 3–4% Sb exploration target — say ≥1.5 Mt at ≥3% Sb — the event the stock has been waiting for since Q1 2026.

Everything is binary and nothing is partially de-risked. The company has drilled 44,000 m in eighteen months and still quotes only a conceptual target; the technical report said a resource by Q1 2026, commentary said July, and no new date has been given. Until an MRE exists there is no NAV, no anchor, and no way to size the prize — only grade and a policy narrative.

Sub-gates
  1. The resource: ≥1.5 Mt classified at ≥3% Sb with Main Zone continuity at 3–5 m widths; a weak MRE from 44,000 m of drilling would be devastating.
  2. Metallurgy: a saleable stibnite concentrate ≥50% Sb; gold deportment (pyrite-hosted → refractory?) and penalty elements (As, Hg, Pb) unknown — no sample has been sent to a lab, as far as disclosed.
  3. Hard dollars: cash C$4.9M at 31 May vs ~C$1.1M a month; the flow-through money cannot pay G&A, permitting consultants or met work.
  4. 27 Nov 2026: China's suspension of the US export ban expires — the one dated price event; renewal or lapse decides whether a Western premium survives.
  5. Permitting: EIA registration and an Indigenous engagement plan still being drafted; the Q4 2026/Q1 2027 application target is unrealistic without a resource.
Product, price & policy lever

Product & processing. Would sell a stibnite (Sb₂S₃) concentrate from gravity and flotation, as the historical Lake George mine did — and nothing about that is proven: the Nov 2025 technical report has no metallurgy, 'detailed metallurgical studies' were promised in May 2026, and no result has been published. No offtake, no qualification, no smelter MOU. Logical buyers would be US Antimony's Montana and Mexico smelters, Campine in Belgium, the US DLA stockpile or Perpetua's planned Idaho plant — none confirmed. Gold deportment (pyrite- or quartz-hosted) is unstudied; if refractory it complicates the flowsheet.

Price. No exchange price. Rotterdam 99.65% metal ~US$19,800/t on 4 Sep 2026 — down 67% from the ~US$59,750 peak of Jul 2025 and still 10–30% above the pre-control baseline; China domestic US$12,875 (+18% on the month); US spot ~US$13/lb (~US$28,700/t) because the US military end-user ban is only suspended. Assessed by Fastmarkets, Asian Metal and SMM; actual trade is bilateral with wide regional spreads (China–Europe 54% in September). For an explorer with production no earlier than 2029–30, today's spot barely matters; what matters is whether a Western premium of US$5–15k/t over China survives.

Policy lever. Three levers, one dated: (1) China's export controls — licensing since Sep 2024, a ban on US exports from Dec 2024, suspended on 9 Nov 2025 until 27 Nov 2026, which is the binary in eight weeks; (2) US money — Perpetua's US$2.9B EXIM loan (Mar 2026) proves the US funds antimony at scale, for a permitted US gold-antimony mine; a West Point advisor was hired in July to open DoD/EXIM doors; nothing received; (3) Canada — antimony on the critical-minerals list, hence the 30% flow-through credit behind the 29 Sep raise, and a ministerial site visit in June. The Chinese controls are durable; the suspension is not; the US premium lasts only while the US refuses Chinese metal.

MetricHeadlineDetail
Risk score5/5The only 5 in the file. Nothing limits permanent loss except the grade in the ground and the possibility of a resource: no MRE, no metallurgy, no study, under a year of cash, antimony −67% from peak, a missed resource date, a 3.5% royalty, insiders ~3%, and a promotional investor-relations footprint. Not a 4 because there is no partial de-risking anywhere — every value driver is still binary. The loss path: the MRE slips again or classifies under 1 Mt → the C$0.22 low breaks → a hard-dollar raise at C$0.15–0.20 with full warrants → 50%+ dilution; or antimony settles at US$10–12k/t and Western funding goes only to Perpetua and US Antimony, leaving a Canadian explorer unfinanceable and the Globex option lapsing.
Market cap2 Oct 2026: ~US$35M (C$0.405 · ~C$49.5M on 122.3M shares). ~US$34M / C$48.7M (C$0.40 on 30 Sep 2026; 121.8M basic; 52-wk C$0.22–1.65).EV ~C$44–48M. From ~C$0.10 (Jul 2025 placement) to C$1.65 (17 Mar 2026) on the antimony spike plus high-grade hits, then −76% as the price mean-reverted, the MRE failed to appear, ~21M placement shares came free in June and the runway shortened. September alone −32%. Share count +118% in a year. Warrants: 22.7M at C$0.75 to Dec 2027 — far out of the money; up to 10.6M FT shares at C$0.47 pending; FD ~155M+ (options undisclosed).
Capex / IRRNo economic study; no capex.Deposit geometry (narrow, steep stibnite veins) implies an underground mine, as at Lake George. Illustrative only, not a NAV: if an MRE confirmed ~2.7 Mt @ 3.5% and a later study showed ~250 kt/yr at 85% recovery and 80% payability, that is ~7,600 t of payable Sb a year — ~US$137M of revenue at US$18,000/t for ~11 years; whether that carries C$100–150M of capex and New Brunswick underground costs is unknowable now, and at US$10,000/t it very likely does not.
NPVNo NPV. In-situ value of the target at the 3.5% mid-case (94.5 kt Sb): US$0.95B at US$10,000/t · US$1.7B at US$18,000 · US$2.8B at US$30,000 · US$4.7B at US$50,000.Current EV is 3.3% / 1.8% / 1.1% / 0.65% of those in-situ values. The honest working position: at C$49M the market prices Bald Hill as a coin-flip on delivering a 2–3 Mt @ 3%+ resource with a Western-premium price — and all three conditions (tonnes, continuity, price) are unresolved. A P/NAV multiple cannot be computed honestly at this stage.
DepositExploration target 2.71 Mt @ 3–4% Sb (81–108 kt), Main Zone, 0.5% cut-off — 'conceptual in nature'.Main Zone: NW-trending stibnite veins and breccias, 600–700 m strike × 350–400 m depth, 3–5 m average widths, open all directions. Massive-stibnite pods give the headlines — 36.0% (BHW-26-04), 33.4%, 27.3%/0.3 m — inside 3–11 m zones at 2–4%; rich, narrow and nuggety, which is exactly what makes classification hard. Central Zone (BHC-26-01: 10.3 m @ 0.32% incl. 2.2 m @ 2.63%), South Zone (trench grabs to 44% Sb), Marcus Zone (new), Second Run soils 3 km south. Discovered 2008 (4.51 m @ 11.7%).
NPV : capexn/a — no study, no resource.The screen that applies today is EV per tonne of eventual classified antimony versus peers, and the answer depends entirely on how much of the 2.7 Mt survives an MRE.
Valuation Anchor

~US$220–300 per tonne of antimony in the exploration target (EV ~US$24M at C$0.30, 15 Oct ÷ 81–108 kt) — 1.1–1.5% of in-situ value at a Rotterdam price of ~US$19,800/t, ~1.7–2.3% at China's ~US$12,900. Cheap-looking, with the caveat that makes it meaningless: the denominator is conceptual. If an MRE classifies 2 Mt at 3.5% this is one of the cheapest antimony resources anywhere; if it classifies 0.8 Mt it is expensive. The number cannot be read until the resource exists.

The case: the MRE lands at ≥1.5 Mt @ 3%+, metallurgy shows a clean concentrate, and China lets its suspension lapse on 27 Nov — a 2–3× from C$0.40 on narrative and scarcity, before the next raise. The bear case is the base rate for a company at this stage: the MRE disappoints or slips again, the hard-dollar raise prices below C$0.40 with full warrants, and the position compounds down. Size of prize even in success is modest in absolute terms — a 2.7 Mt @ 3.5% underground mine is a US$100–150M-revenue operation, a C$200–400M company in a good antimony market, 4–8× before dilution. Not a Perpetua.

Bucket: grade / discovery / optionality — the pure form of it. A position sized for a zero.

Shareholder structure
Management & board
~3.4%. CEO Jim Atkinson ~1.97M (1.6%); director Rodney Stevens 1.41M (1.2%); CFO Sheryl Jones 125k; Jagtar Sandhu ~650k after his sale (pre-/post-sale status unclear). A three-person board with no independent technical director, no mine-builder, no permitting or Indigenous-relations executive; the CEO is also the sole QP.
Insider activity (12 mo)
Net selling at the top. Jagtar Sandhu sold ~C$2.7M of stock in Feb 2026 — 84% of his direct holding — a month before the C$1.65 peak. No insider purchases found in 2026. SEDI not fetched directly.
Strategic & prominent
No producer, smelter or government holder. An unnamed 'strategic resource investor' took 5.75M shares at C$0.10 in Jul 2025 (~4.7%, identity undisclosed). 1832 Asset Management (Dynamic Funds) 6.5% — the only institution found; whether it still holds after the collapse is unknown. Globex is owed up to 1.1M shares under the option.
Institutions / retail
Overwhelmingly retail, with a heavy German/Frankfurt following (IRW-Press distribution, commissioned GBC research with a C$2.66 'fair value', promotional commentary) and US retail from the Dec 2025 round (Dominari/Revere; 8% cash + 8% broker warrants). 121.8M shares; 22.7M warrants at C$0.75; FD ~155M+.

Read: No anchor, no strategic, ~3% insiders and the one large early holder already cashed out near the top. The register is a 2025–26 momentum crowd now underwater against the C$0.45 December round, with warrants far out of the money so no warrant cash is coming. Expect it to churn on each financing. The paid landing page, commissioned research and German pump-style coverage are themselves a yellow flag on how this stock is marketed.

Jurisdiction risk

National: Canada, Tier-1. Antimony is on Canada's critical-minerals list, which is why the 29 Sep raise is flow-through (30% exploration tax credit). A federal Impact Assessment trigger is 'unclear' per the company — a small underground antimony mine probably falls below the thresholds.

Provincial: New Brunswick, pro-mining with history. Mining Act and Clean Environment Act EIA registration; the company began baseline surveys, EIA scoping, stormwater modelling and archaeology with GEMTEC in spring 2026 and an SRK gap analysis; it targets a permit application in Q4 2026/Q1 2027, which is ambitious with no resource. The Natural Resources minister visited the site on 4 Jun 2026. NB hosted North America's last antimony mine (Lake George, intermittently 1970s–90s) — the CEO ran its geology.

Local / Indigenous: the open layer. The company has met NB's Department of Indigenous Affairs to identify the groups and is drafting an engagement plan; no agreement. The Wolastoqey Nation's Aboriginal-title claim (filed 2020–21) covers much of southern and central NB including Queens County Crown lands, with Mi'kmaq and Peskotomuhkati interests overlapping — unresolved title litigation is the main non-technical permitting risk in the province (general knowledge, not from company documents). The Globex 3.5% gross royalty is heavy for a narrow-vein mine.

Differentiator

Grade. 3–4% Sb average with massive-stibnite lenses to 44% is top-decile globally — Larvotto's Hillgrove runs ~1.1% Sb plus gold; Perpetua's Stibnite ~0.06% Sb with 4.8 Moz of gold. Narrow, but very rich.

  1. Jurisdiction and logistics — Canada, highway, port 55 km, power, a province actively courting it, and antimony mining heritage in the same district.
  2. Policy tailwind — antimony on the US, EU and Canadian critical lists; a US military end-user ban (suspended) and DoD stockpiling that create a Western premium a Canadian producer could capture.
  3. Exploration room — three new zones, visible gold, 37 km² with fresh geophysics, soils 3 km south.

Counterweight: all of it is pre-resource; the Main Zone is a vein system whose continuity between bonanza pods is unproven; no metallurgy; the company has missed its own MRE timetable by two quarters; insiders own ~3% and a founder-level holder sold 84% near the top; the antimony price that made the story has already mean-reverted. The grade is real; everything that would turn it into value is still ahead.

Exploration upside

Exploration is the whole story · moves the needle: high, in both directions. 19,000 m in 2026 (13,000 m Main Zone infill/extension; 6,000 m on Central, South and Marcus), 1,500+ soils over 37 km², a 594 line-km aeromag/VLF-EM survey (completed Aug, interpretation pending). What would move it: Main Zone continuity below 350 m and along strike (BH-26-21 at 144–153 m is shallow); Central turning from 0.3% over 10 m into a second zone; gold assays replicating the 285 g/t XRF spot read; Second Run becoming a second deposit. The 'high' needle cuts both ways — a weak MRE from this drill density would be devastating.

Next Catalyst

Oct–Nov: remaining 2026 assays (Central, South, Marcus) and the airborne interpretation; 27 Nov 2026: China's US-export-ban suspension expires — the one dated price binary; ~Dec: FY results (cash at 31 Aug); Q4 2026–Q1 2027: the maiden MRE — originally Q1 2026, then 'July', now undated; Q4 2026/Q1 2027: the company's permit-application target; 2027: first metallurgy, a hard-dollar raise, a PEA at the earliest; Dec 2027: 22.7M C$0.75 warrants expire; Jan 2028 / Jan 2029: Globex payments.

Open Questions
  • Why has the MRE slipped from Q1 2026 to 'July' to no date after 44,000 m — continuity and classification, or a choice to drill more first?
  • What is cash at 31 Aug, did the C$5M flow-through close, with which agent and fees, and is a hard-dollar round coming at or below C$0.40?
  • Has any metallurgical sample gone to a lab? What is the gold deportment, and does the concentrate carry penalty elements?
  • Which First Nations have been identified under the engagement plan, and does the Wolastoqey title claim cover the Bald Hill claims?
  • Who is the Jul 2025 'strategic resource investor', and does 1832/Dynamic still hold 6.5%?
  • Has the US Section 232 outcome created any procurement or financing path for a Canadian antimony explorer, or only for US projects?
Context & updates
23 Oct 2026 update

The C$3.0M unit placement closed on 23 Oct (10M units at C$0.30, half a C$0.50 warrant to Oct 2028, C$180k finder fee, ~C$2.8M net). The release does not name the "strategic mining investor", so the outside validation the 17 Oct note hoped for has not come. The units are free-trading (LIFE exemption, no hold period), and the shares closed at C$0.28 on 23 Oct, below the C$0.30 unit price. Share count ~132.9M → ~142.9M (+7.5%; up to ~147.9M with the warrants). Overhead is now covered for about three quarters; a bigger hard-dollar raise is still needed before metallurgy or a study. Odds of a 3× 9% → 10% on the lower price (−12% since 16 Oct); risk 5 unchanged.

17 Oct 2026 update

The C$3.0M unit placement announced on 15 Oct is fully subscribed by a single strategic mining investor (16 Oct release), closing on or about 21 Oct. Who it is matters more than the amount: a producer or a Western offtaker would be the first outside validation of Bald Hill; a fund dressed as strategic would not. Odds and risk unchanged until the closing release names the investor.

15 Oct 2026 update

A hard-dollar raise is announced: up to C$3.0M in 10M units at C$0.30 with half a C$0.50 two-year warrant (LIFE, non-brokered, closing about 21 Oct). It answers the overhead question for about three quarters, but it is smaller than the ≥C$5M the gate asked for and priced below the C$0.40 floor — the flow-through two weeks earlier was at C$0.47. Dilution ~7.5% now, up to ~11% with the warrants. Cash and funding rows updated; the cash chip changes once the money closes. The 14 Oct assays (BHW-26-19 4.35% Sb over 13 m) are infill for the mineral model; the restart with two rigs is promised in about two weeks, still with no resource date. Odds and risk unchanged: more money at a lower price nets out to about the same per-share picture.

5 Oct 2026 update

The C$5.0M flow-through closed on 5 Oct (10.64M shares at C$0.47; broker Castlewood Capital, C$245k cash fee and 0.52M broker warrants at C$0.75). That adds ~C$4.75M net for Bald Hill exploration and lifts the share count ~8.7% to ~132.9M, so the market value rises to ~C$54M at the unchanged price while net cash rises by about the same amount. Nothing else changes: flow-through money cannot pay G&A, permitting or metallurgy, so a hard-dollar raise is still needed within about two quarters, and there is still no MRE date. Odds unchanged.

3 Oct 2026 refresh

The C$5M flow-through announced 29 Sep hasn't closed; no MRE date and no new assays. Rotterdam antimony ~US$19,000–19,500/t on 2 Oct. China's US-export-ban suspension (27 Nov) may have rolled to 10 Jan 2027 with the truce — not confirmed for antimony.

China's suspension of the US export ban expires 27 Nov 2026

China suspended (not lifted) its Dec 2024 ban on antimony, gallium and germanium exports to the US on 9 Nov 2025, until 27 Nov 2026; the Sep 2024 licensing controls remain. Renewal or lapse is the biggest dated price catalyst for every Western antimony name.

Antimony has mean-reverted

Rotterdam 99.65% ~US$19,800/t on 4 Sep 2026 against ~US$59,750 at the Jul 2025 peak (−67%); China domestic US$12,875 (+18% m/m after three down months); US spot still ~US$13/lb because the military end-user ban is only suspended. Commentary expects ~US$10/lb Western by end-2026.

Perpetua sets the Western funding template (Mar 2026)

US$2.9B EXIM loan for Stibnite (US$2.4B principal, 13 years, available H2 2026) — proof the US will finance antimony at scale, and that it does so for a permitted US gold-antimony mine with 148.7 Mlb of Sb reserves, not for a pre-resource Canadian explorer.

The resource that hasn't come

Nov 2025 technical report: maiden MRE 'by Q1 2026'. German commentary cited 23 Jul 2026 after an 18,000 m program. The 11 Sep release says drilling is 'filling data gaps using the updated mineral model' — infill toward a resource, with no date; the 29 Sep financing release still quotes only the conceptual 2.7 Mt.

The corporate side is a renamed CSE shell with a serial-promoter origin; the project optionality (Globex option) means they do not yet own Bald Hill outright.

Stallion Uranium U₃O₈

TSXV: STUD · OTCQB: STLNF website
US$12MC$0.125 · 23 Oct 2026 (~C$19M)Risk 4/5
Rankings: A #9 · B #6 of 9 · 3×+ odds ~10%rankings ↗
Value / optionalityFunded to catalyst2nd 3×: partlyCash: ~12 monthsMgmt: C
No resource, no study, no discovery — a first-pass drill program (4,585 m, 6 holes) with alteration, structure and weak radioactivity; no assay published yet

A C$19M explorer (16 Oct) with ~C$13.7M of cash at mid-year and the largest contiguous land package in the western Athabasca — the market pays under C$10M for 1,700 km² next to Arrow and Triple R, because the first six holes ever drilled at Coyote found the right rocks and no uranium worth naming

Gate A discovery hole: an assayed intercept at Coyote (or Stone Island) that an Athabasca geologist would call mineralisation — metres above 0.5% U₃O₈ at or near the unconformity, not a 2.3 m band at 595 cps. Sub-gates: (1) the remaining ~2,165 m of Phase II (Q4 2026) hit the Target A–E gravity-low / conductor intersections rather than more alteration; (2) assays for ML26-001A to -006A are published and show uranium, not just pathfinders; (3) a follow-up program is funded without a raise below the C$0.26 warrant strike; (4) the ATHA option is kept alive — C$12M of Saskatchewan exploration credits by ~July 2028 for 70% of the optioned claims; (5) a 2027 program that steps out from a mineralised hole, not another round of 'textbook setting' holes.

Deposit None. Moonlite project (~1,700 km², SW Athabasca): Coyote corridor — 8.5 km, nine EM conductors, a strong gravity low, best reading so far 2.3 m averaging 595 cps (max 1,416 cps) in ML26-005 · Stone Island, Lynx, Fishhook untested
Athabasca Basin · SaskatchewanExplorer (pre-discovery · first holes 2026)Insiders <1% · one 16% outside holder

Uranium · 100% uranium — no resource, no study, no discovery; western Athabasca land package, first drilling in 2026 · Reviewed Oct 2026

Funding & next catalyst

C$13.69M at 30 Jun 2026 (Q2 balance sheet via StockAnalysis; the Sep 2026 presentation says ~C$13.6M 'treasury'); C$19.96M at 31 Dec 2025, C$16.33M at 31 Mar 2026. Liabilities C$0.97M. 30 Sep cash not yet reported — ~C$10M is my estimate after the summer drilling
Debt
None disclosed (debt/equity ~0 on Yahoo; liabilities C$0.97M at 30 Jun). Flow-through obligation: the C$7.72M raised at C$0.45 in Dec 2025 must be spent on Canadian critical-mineral exploration by 31 Dec 2026 — the Phase II metres and Stone Island work are that spend
Burn · runway
~C$3.6M in Q1 and ~C$2.6M in Q2 2026 (cash deltas; drilling 1,900 m by April, 3,527 m by end-June, 4,626 m by 13 Aug). At ~C$3M a quarter the cash lasts into 2027; a 2027 drill program needs a raise, and the last two were at C$0.20 (Aug–Sep 2025, with full warrants at C$0.26) and C$0.45 (Dec 2025, flow-through) — both above today's C$0.14
Next catalyst
Q4 2026: the remaining ~2,165 m of Phase II at Coyote (recommenced 29 Sep) → assays for the 2026 holes (SRC Saskatoon; none published yet) → Q3 financials by late Nov (30 Sep cash) → Stone Island target ranking → a 2027 program and financing decision, likely Q1 2027 → ATHA option deadline ~Jul 2028
Funded to the next catalyst Funded for the rest of the 2026 program and the Q4 assays with no gap. Not funded for a 2027 program of the same size without a raise — and at C$0.14 with 85M warrants struck at C$0.26, the next raise dilutes hard. The option to walk away from the ATHA claims is real: the C$12M credit requirement runs to ~mid-2028 and progress against it is not disclosed.
Thesis

A land-and-targets story in the right postcode. Moonlite (~1,700 km² / 430,764 acres; 67,613 ha on the project page) sits in the south-western Athabasca Basin — the Patterson Lake corridor that produced NexGen's Arrow (256.7 Mlb M+I), Fission's Triple R (114.9 Mlb Indicated), Orano's Shea Creek and F3's JR zone. The package is a mix of 100%-owned claims and 47 claims (547,524 acres at signing) under option from ATHA Energy: Stallion is sole operator and earns 70% by spending C$12M of Saskatchewan exploration-expenditure credits within 60 months of the July 2023 definitive agreement (C$3.31M in the first year, the rest by ~July 2028); ATHA keeps 30% including a legacy carried interest. The package has shrunk from 715,450 acres in 2023 to 430,764 — which claims were dropped is not disclosed.

The flagship target is Coyote: an 8.5 km corridor on 100%-owned ground with nine ground-EM conductors (six above 10 S), a gravity low the company calls 'one of the strongest ground gravity anomalies in the Athabasca Basin', basement structure at NE/NW intersections, unconformity at ~450 m. It had never been drilled. The maiden program (Feb 2026, two rigs, Base Drilling) was planned at 4,000 m, expanded to 5,500 m in May and 6,750 m in August; 4,585 m were done by 29 Sep. Every hole hit wide fault zones, quartz dissolution, brecciation, clay and graphitic faulting — ML26-001A two fault zones of 66–109 m — and 'elevated radioactivity' above 300 cps. The best so far: ML26-005, 2.3 m averaging 595 cps (max 1,416 cps) at 572–574 m, plus 0.3 m at 648 cps; ML26-006A 0.3 m at 458 cps. For scale, ore-grade Athabasca intercepts run tens of thousands of cps. No assay from any 2026 hole has been published; samples went to SRC in May. Two holes were lost (ML26-002, -002A); a serious injury to two contractors in April paused the program for three weeks.

Second target Stone Island (100%-owned, 18 km conductive trend): 676 line-km of VTEM (Feb 2026) and 519 gravity stations (summer) produced three target areas, A being the strongest; untested. Lynx (13 km, next to Orano's Uchrich) and Fishhook (18 km; a historical 0.139% U₃O₈ occurrence nearby, unverified) are untested. The company says all targets are permitted. Horse Heaven (Idaho Au–Sb, next to Perpetua's Stibnite) is gone: sold via an optionee to Resolution Minerals (ASX: RML) in July 2025 for 59.5M RML shares (escrowed in tranches to July 2026), 29.7M RML options at A$0.018 to July 2028 and ~A$145k cash (Stallion's pro rata share per the Jul 2025 release) — a small marketable holding, value not disclosed since. The website still shows Horse Heaven as 100%-owned; that page is stale.

Money: C$15M at C$0.20 in Aug–Sep 2025 (75M units with 75M five-year warrants at C$0.26; Matthew Mason took 15M FT units and became a 20%+ control person) and C$7.72M of flow-through at C$0.45 in Dec 2025. Cash C$13.69M at 30 Jun 2026. CEO Matthew Schwab (Arrow discovery team at NexGen, Hathor/Roughrider; ex-Kraken Energy), VP Exploration Darren Slugoski (Gemini discovery 2021), CFO Paulo Santos (Jan 2026). At C$0.14 (15 Oct) the market cap is C$21M / US$14M — EV roughly C$8–11M for 1,700 km² and a 6,750 m drill program. Uranium: spot ~US$89/lb, term ~US$96 (Sep 2026).

The Gate

A discovery hole: an assayed intercept at Coyote or Stone Island that an Athabasca geologist would call mineralisation — metres above 0.5% U₃O₈ at or near the unconformity, not a 2.3 m band at 595 cps.

Six holes into a never-drilled corridor have confirmed the plumbing — faults, alteration, graphite, a little gamma — and nothing else. That is normal for a first pass in the Athabasca, where discoveries typically come on hole 10–40, and it is also what most failed programs look like. The company has twice expanded the program on 'alteration and structure', which is a vote of confidence and also the usual way an explorer spends its flow-through. The assays are the first hard number; the remaining 2,165 m are the first targeted test of the gravity-low / conductor intersections (Targets A–E) rather than the corridor at large.

Sub-gates
  1. Phase II metres (Q4 2026): the 2,165 m go into Targets A–E; the test is a hole with sustained radioactivity in the thousands of cps, not another 'textbook setting' release.
  2. Assays: ML26-001A to -006A at SRC — published, with uranium ppm, not just 'elevated radioactivity'. Nothing has been published since drilling began in February.
  3. Financing without a down-round: a 2027 program needs ~C$8–10M; the last prices were C$0.20 and C$0.45, the stock is C$0.14, and 85M warrants sit at C$0.26.
  4. ATHA option: C$12M of credits by ~July 2028 — progress not disclosed; dropping the option would halve the land story.
  5. A step-out, not a restart: 2027 drilling that follows a mineralised hole; Stone Island A–C and Lynx remain back-ups.
Product, price & policy lever

Product & processing. Nothing to sell and nothing designed. If a deposit were found in this corridor the development path is known — Arrow and Triple R are both permitting underground mines with their own mills 30–60 km from Stallion's ground, Cameco's Blind River/Port Hope convert the yellowcake — but Stallion is a decade and several hundred million dollars from that. The realistic exit, if there is a discovery, is a takeout by NexGen, Paladin (Fission), Cameco/Orano or F3-style money long before any study.

Price. Spot ~US$89/lb (29 Sep 2026), long-term ~US$96–96.50 (10 Sep), term above spot — the same backdrop as the rest of the uranium tracker. For a pre-discovery explorer the price matters only through sentiment and financing windows: Stallion raised C$22.7M in 2025 at C$0.20–0.45 when the sector was bid, and is now at C$0.14 with the sector stronger — the discount is company-specific (no result), not price-driven.

Policy lever. (1) Canadian flow-through with the critical-mineral credit — the Dec 2025 C$7.72M at C$0.45 was priced at a large premium because of it; taxpayer-subsidised drilling, already received, and the reason the metres must be spent by 31 Dec 2026. (2) Saskatchewan's exploration-expenditure credits are the currency of the ATHA option. (3) The US ban on Russian uranium (waivers end 1 Jan 2028) helps the sector's narrative, not an explorer's cash flow. No government money and none expected.

A discovery here would be worth more per pound than one in Nunavut because the infrastructure and the buyers are next door — but that is a statement about the neighbourhood, not about Stallion.

A barren program is worth cash minus a year's burn: ~0.4×. The in-situ-capture arithmetic used for ATHA does not apply — there is nothing in situ.

Valuation Anchor

Cash ~C$10–13.7M against a C$21M cap (C$0.14, 15 Oct): the market pays ~C$8–11M (US$6–8M) for 1,700 km² in the western Athabasca, a 6,750 m maiden program two-thirds done, and three more untested corridors. That is cheap by the yardstick of land and targets and fair by the yardstick of results, which is none. Western Athabasca explorers without a discovery (Purepoint, Baselode, Forum's basin ground) trade in the same US$10–30M band; those with one (F3 after JR, IsoEnergy after Hurricane) re-rated to US$100M+ within months.

The case: the Target A–E holes in Q4 or a 2027 step-out return an assayed intercept of metres above 0.5% U₃O₈ — the stock is US$50–150M (3–10×) before the follow-up raise, and the Patterson Lake neighbours become bidders. The bear case: the remaining 2,165 m and the assays confirm alteration and pathfinders only, the company spends 2027 on Stone Island with a raise at C$0.10–0.12, and the stock drifts to cash value. The nearest hard test is the Q4 metres plus the assays; nothing before that is more than geophysics.

The policy backdrop (flow-through, critical-mineral credits, Saskatchewan) is why an explorer with no result can still raise C$22.7M in a year — and why the metres have to be drilled by December whether or not the first holes argued for it.

Bucket: value / optionality — cash-backed, in the best uranium jurisdiction, with nothing found.

Shareholder structure
Management & board
~17% insiders on Simply Wall St (16.7% individual insiders, almost all one holder); the Sep 2026 presentation claims 'insider/management ~45%' — the two cannot both be right, and the higher number likely counts friends-and-family placement buyers. CEO Matthew Schwab (NexGen Arrow discovery team 2014, Hathor/Roughrider, ex-Kraken Energy CEO), CFO Paulo Santos (ex-Calibre, Elevation Gold; appointed Jan 2026), VP Exploration Darren Slugoski (Gemini discovery 2021, Spitfire), directors Peter Dembicki (Tier One Silver), Terri Anne Welyki, Drew Zimmerman (prior CEO). Options 3.9M.
Insider activity (12 mo)
Not disclosed in sources reached. Simply Wall St shows insufficient data; SEDI could not be fetched. The only insider transaction on record is the Aug–Sep 2025 placement, in which Matthew Mason bought 15M FT units at C$0.20 and became a control person.
Strategic & prominent
Matthew Mason 16.4% (24.6M shares; a 20%+ control person at the Sep 2025 closing, since diluted) — a private investor, background not disclosed. ATHA Energy holds 3,333,333 pre-consolidation shares (~0.67M post-5:1) from the 2023 option and a 30% residual on the optioned claims. Resolution Minerals (ASX: RML) paper is an asset, not a holder.
Institutions / retail
Institutions ~13.7% — Vident 4.0%, ALPS/Sprott URNM 2.9%, BetaShares 2.4%, Middlefield 1.5%; retail ~70%. 150.13M shares; FD ~235–239M with 85M warrants at C$0.26 and 3.9M options.

Read: A retail register with one large private holder who paid C$0.20 and is under water, and a thin ETF tail that will follow uranium flows. Management owns little that is disclosed; nobody has been reported buying at C$0.15–0.22. The 85M warrants at C$0.26 cap the stock until a result moves it through the strike, and then supply the next C$22M. Nothing here says the people closest to the core know something; nothing says they are adding either.

Jurisdiction risk

Canada, Tier-1 — Saskatchewan, the best uranium jurisdiction in the world. Moonlite sits in the south-western Athabasca on the Patterson Lake corridor, with Highway 955 and the Cluff Lake road, provincial power at Patterson Lake, and two projects (Arrow, Triple R) that have been through a provincial EA and are in CNSC licensing — the regulatory path for a discovery here is mapped and the province wants it used. Claims are provincial mineral claims with annual expenditure requirements; the company says all current targets are permitted for drilling. First Nations: the corridor is in the traditional territory of the Clearwater River Dene Nation and Métis communities with whom NexGen and Paladin have signed benefit agreements; Stallion discloses no agreements, which is normal at the grassroots stage. The one jurisdiction event of 2026 was the company's own: a serious injury to two Base Drilling contractors on 8 Apr, a three-week stop, and a Chief Mines Inspector sign-off on 28 Apr.

The policy backdrop — flow-through with the critical-mineral credit, the Saskatchewan exploration credit that drives the ATHA option, the US ban on Russian uranium from 2028 — favours Saskatchewan explorers in financing windows. It does not find uranium.

Differentiator

Address and price. The largest contiguous land package in the western Athabasca, on the trend that produced the basin's last three major discoveries, priced at about the cash on hand — a cheap call option on a discovery hole, which is what every pre-discovery explorer sells and few sell this cheaply.

  1. Targeting depth — three years of layered geophysics (VTEM, MobileMT, ground TDEM, two gravity phases, a 3D inversion, SRK structural study) before the first hole; the Target A–E model is a real Athabasca targeting exercise, not a line of holes along a conductor.
  2. People who have done it — the CEO was on the Arrow team and at Hathor; the VP Exploration was on the Gemini discovery. Discovery pedigree is the one thing that can be bought with a C$21M cap.
  3. Pipeline — Stone Island (three targets), Lynx (next to Orano), Fishhook and six further corridors; if Coyote disappoints there is a second and third act on the same permits.

Counterweight: six holes, two lost, no assay, best reading 1,416 cps; the program has been expanded twice on alteration, not grade; the 2025 financings at C$0.20 and C$0.45 are both under water, so the next raise is a down-round unless Q4 delivers; the land package has quietly shrunk 40% since 2023; and the ATHA 70% is still an option, not an interest. A well-run grassroots explorer at a grassroots price — the stock is cheap because the rocks have not said anything yet.

Exploration upside

The entire thesis · moves the needle: high. Q4 2026: ~2,165 m at Coyote into Target Areas A–E (gravity low / conductor / structure intersections, unconformity ~450 m), the first targeted test after six reconnaissance holes; assays for all 2026 holes. 2027: a follow-up at Coyote if anything assays, otherwise Stone Island A–C (first drilling), Lynx, Fishhook — all permitted. What moves the valuation is one thing — a hole with sustained radioactivity in the thousands of cps and an assay above 0.5% U₃O₈.

Next Catalyst

Q4 2026: Phase II completion — ~2,165 m at Coyote (recommenced 29 Sep) with radiometric updates as holes finish; Q4 2026–Q1 2027: assays for ML26-001A to -006A and the Phase II holes (SRC) — none published to date; 17 Oct: CEO at the Schachter Catch the Energy conference, Calgary; late Nov: Q3 financials (30 Sep cash; 30 Jun was C$13.69M); 31 Dec 2026: flow-through spending deadline; Q1 2027: 2027 program and financing; ~Jul 2028: ATHA option deadline (C$12M of credits).

Open Questions
  • What do the 2026 holes assay — samples went to SRC in May and nothing has been published?
  • How much of the C$12M ATHA credit requirement has been met, and how much of the 1,700 km² is optioned versus owned?
  • Why did the land package fall from 715,450 acres (2023) to 430,764 (2026) — lapsed ATHA claims or Stallion's own?
  • Who is Matthew Mason, and does he support the next raise?
  • What is the Resolution Minerals holding worth now, and has any of it been sold?
  • Does Stallion still hold anything in Nevada or elsewhere outside Saskatchewan — the 2023 release mentioned Idaho and Nevada gold projects?
  • What did the Q2 MD&A say about 2026 budget and the 2027 plan — the PDF could not be retrieved?
Context & updates
5 Oct 2026 — card created

Priced at C$0.155 (5 Oct close, Google Finance; 2 Oct C$0.135, 30 Sep C$0.22). 150.13M shares, cash C$13.69M at 30 Jun. No resource, no discovery, no assays. Bucket value / optionality; risk 4/5.

Phase II recommenced (29 Sep 2026)

4,585 m done of 6,750 m; ~2,165 m remain, directed by an SRK structural study and the Target A–E gravity model. Core measured with an RS-125 spectrometer; samples to SRC. No new numbers.

Highest radioactivity to date (13 Aug 2026)

ML26-005: 2.3 m averaging 595 cps, max 1,416 cps, at 571.9–574.2 m; 0.3 m at 648 cps at 650 m. ML26-006A: 0.3 m at 458 cps. 4,626 m drilled (84% of 5,500 m); program expanded to 6,750 m. Gravity survey mobilised to Stone Island. These are weak readings by Athabasca standards.

Stone Island becomes a second target (7 Jul · 3 Sep 2026)

676 line-km of VTEM defined a broad conductive corridor with four priority areas; 519 gravity stations narrowed it to three (A strongest). Untested; drilling 'subject to interpretation, permitting and approval'.

Maiden drilling and the April stop (Feb–Jun 2026)

Started 12 Feb (4,000 m, two rigs). Two holes lost. A serious injury to two contractors on 8 Apr stopped the program; the Chief Mines Inspector approved a restart on 28 Apr with ~1,900 m done. Expanded to 5,500 m on 27 May; 3,527 m and three holes with >300 cps by 30 Jun. Samples sent for assay in May.

C$22.7M raised in 2025; Horse Heaven sold (Jul–Dec 2025)

C$15M at C$0.20 in units with five-year C$0.26 warrants (Aug–Sep 2025; Matthew Mason 15M FT units, control person); C$7.72M of flow-through at C$0.45 (30 Dec 2025), to be spent by 31 Dec 2026. Horse Heaven (Idaho) sold to Resolution Minerals on 30 Jul 2025 for 59.5M RML shares, 29.7M options and ~A$145k cash. Shares consolidated 5:1 in Feb 2025.

Management review
Creal weaknesses: slipped dates, thin fit or thin candourTrack record 2/5 · Fit 3/5 · Candour 2/5 · Skin in the game 1/5

The CEO and VP Exploration fit the western-Athabasca job (NexGen Arrow team, Hathor), but the company took a cease-trade order in 2025 for late filings, disclosed an unapproved marketing deal 17 months late, and promotes scintillometer counts with no assay from nine months of drilling. No chair named; CEO owns ~0.2%.

Effect on the odds: chance it works 12% → 10%, odds of a 3× 9% → 8%.

People
Matthew Schwab (CEO & Director, since CEO 29 Nov 2024 (technical advisor from Jan 2024)): Exploration geologist, Saskatoon. Senior exploration geologist at NexGen during the 2014 Arrow discovery (company says 'instrumental'; independent credit not verified); 'development team' member at Hathor before Roughrider sale to Rio Tinto (C$654M, 2012); co-founder/SVP Axiom Exploration (private consultancy); CEO of Kraken Energy (CSE: UUSA, US uranium) Aug 2022 – Sep 2024, resigned without stated reason. First TSXV CEO role is Kraken; second is Stallion. · (no chair identified) (Board: Drew Zimmerman (ex-CEO, director), Peter Dembicki, Terri Anne Welyki, since Zimmerman on board since ~2020 (Hybrid Minerals era); Dembicki Sep 2025; Welyki unverified): Company site lists no chairman. Zimmerman: CFA, ex-broker/derivatives PM, ran Stallion as CEO through the 2023 pivot to uranium and the 2024 drill program; no mining operating record. Dembicki: ex-Canaccord corporate finance, currently President & CEO of Tier One Silver (TSXV: TSLV). · Darren Slugoski (VP Exploration Canada, since 2023 (appointed when company was Stallion Discoveries)): B.Sc. Hons geology (U of Sask); 10+ years Athabasca exploration incl. Spitfire deposit (western basin / Patterson Lake trend). Technical lead on the ground; no discovery credited to him as lead. · Paulo Santos, CPA (CFO, since 5 Jan 2026 (replaced Dong Shim, CFO since 2020)): Ex-Elevation Gold, Calibre, Northern Empire, Newmarket Gold, StrikePoint. Hired after the 2025 late-filing cease-trade order.
Track record
  • Schwab's record is as a team geologist on two Athabasca wins (Roughrider, Arrow), not as the leader of either; both wins came in the 2008–2014 uranium cyclesource
  • Schwab's one prior CEO job, Kraken Energy (US uranium, CSE), lasted ~2 years and ended with his resignation as CEO and director in Sep 2024, no discovery or exitsource
  • Prior CEO Zimmerman (broker background) rebranded Hybrid Minerals -> Stallion Discoveries -> Stallion Uranium (Oct 2023); 2024 maiden program: 3 holes / 2,798 m vs 3,300 m planned, best assay 14 ppm Usource
  • No drilling in 2025; year spent on a cease-trade order (7 May – 7 Jul 2025), surveys, and three financings at C$0.10, C$0.20 and C$0.45source
  • 2026: first program under Schwab at Coyote, 4,000 m plan expanded to 6,750 m; ~4,585 m drilled in Phase I, Phase II restarted 29 Sep 2026; no assays published as of Oct 2026source
  • Horse Heaven gold-antimony (Idaho) side project optioned Nov 2024, then exited Jul 2025 via sale of the holding company to ASX-listed Resolution Minerals for RML shares/options and ~A$145k cashsource

Boards today: Schwab: Stallion only (1) as far as found. Zimmerman: Stallion (1). Dembicki: Stallion + CEO of Tier One Silver (2). Welyki: Stallion (1) as far as found.

Fit to this job
Stage and region match: Schwab and Slugoski have both worked basement-hosted unconformity targets in the western Athabasca (Arrow, Spitfire, Shea Creek area), which is exactly the Coyote/Appaloosa job. What is new: Schwab has never led a team to a discovery as CEO; his Kraken stint was in US sandstone uranium and ended without result. Board has no geologist other than the CEO and no one who has taken a uranium project past discovery. Fit is good for the current grassroots-drilling task, unproven for anything beyond it.
Skin & pay
1/5 — under ~0.5%, options only, or net sellers while promoting. Individual insiders 16.7% per Simply Wall St, but 16.4% of that is outside investor Matthew Mason (24.6M shares; bought 4M at C$0.10 Apr 2025 and 15M FT units at C$0.20 Sep 2025, became >20% control person subject to shareholder approval). CEO Schwab ~0.2% (~US$28k); bought 250,000 shares for C$25,000 in the Apr 2025 placement; no open-market buys or sells found (Yahoo/SEDI pages not fetchable). CEO total comp C$195k (90% salary, 10% bonus) vs market cap ~US$14M — modest, not a tell. Institutions ~14% (Vident 4.0%, ALPS/Sprott ETF 2.9%, BetaShares 2.4%). 2026 information circular not fetched. Skin in the game is a signal of the team's own conviction: it never moves the odds, but breaks near-ties in list B of the rankings.

Red flags
  • BCSC cease trade order May–Jul 2025 for failing to file FY2024 financials; two-month trading halt at a company with ~C$20M market cap.
  • Marketing contract (Winning Media, Feb 2024) not submitted to TSXV for approval; disclosed 17 months later inside an unrelated release.
  • Headline pattern: radioactivity counts and 'uranium presence' promoted; the only assays ever published (2024) were background (max 14 ppm U). No 2026 assays after ~9 months of drilling.
  • CEO's prior CEO role (Kraken Energy) ended in resignation after ~2 years with nothing delivered; CEO ownership ~0.2%.
  • Largest holder (Matthew Mason, ~16%) is an outside investor, not management; management and board collectively hold little stock.

What the numbers say
  1. Cash was C$13.69M at 30 June 2026 plus C$2.38M of securities; at about C$3.5M a quarter that is roughly 12-14 months of runway.
  2. H1 2026 put C$5.74M into the ground against C$1.78M of overhead, about 76% ground; in 2025 overhead exceeded exploration.
  3. Shares went from 30.3M to 147.6M in one year after C$24.2M of raises in 2025, with 79.6M warrants still outstanding.
  4. Reported results are swung by non-cash items: flow-through premiums, a C$4.18M stake-sale gain and C$17.7M of impairments in 2024.
  5. No debt; the company was under a cease trade order for late filing in mid-2025, and related-party pay was C$0.65M in H1 2026.

All figures in C$ millions. Statements are reported in whole dollars; figures are shown to TWO decimals because several quarters (2024-H1 2025) have cash, spending and losses well under C$1M and one decimal would show mostly zeros. Share counts are on the post-consolidation basis (1-for-5 consolidation effective 28 Feb 2025, retroactively restated in the FY2024 audited and later statements). 'cash' excludes short-term investments (marketable securities), which are listed separately in notes.

How to read a card. The bucket colour on the left is the archetype. The gate is the single thing that has to go right; the differentiator is what the company has that peers don't. For critical minerals, also read which processing step is missing and which policy lever the price depends on.

Many of these markets are thin, opaque or set by policy; resource anchors use different units by mineral (t LCE, lb U3O8, t contained graphite, kg NdPr). This is research shorthand, not investment advice.

Human judgement on what matters. Machine effort on everything else. I set the rules; Claude, an AI by Anthropic, does the homework every night.

Not investment advice. Personal research notes by Chung Huynh — views, rankings and estimates may be wrong or out of date. Do your own research.
